## 2026-08-06 — UI-Foundation.9: DockControl shell, icon toolbar, status bar

Rewrote `MainWindow.axaml` around a `Dock.Avalonia` `DockControl` (`MainDock`) inside a `DockPanel`: a real File/Edit/View/Connections/Run menu, a `Material.Icons.Avalonia` icon toolbar (New/Open/Save, Run/Cancel/Copy Result/Reset Session), and a bottom status bar carrying `WorkerState.Current` and `ActiveDocument.SessionStateDisplay`. The old fixed `Grid` + `GridSplitter` layout, the inline `NativeWebView`, and the inline results `TabControl` are gone — the docking layout now comes entirely from `DockFactory` (Task 6).

Moved the Results/Output/Problems/Schema panel into a new `ResultsView` `UserControl`, which also closes two long-standing gaps: the `Problems` tab is now bound to `ShellViewModel.Problems` (double-tapping a row calls `ProblemViewModel.Navigate`), and a `Schema` list is bound to `Result.Schema` — both data sets have existed since Milestone A/B but had empty placeholder tabs until now.

Binding the Problems tab exposed a real defect that the first cut of this task shipped and code review caught: `ShellViewModel.Problems` is a derived, recomputed-on-access CLR property, and **nothing raised `PropertyChanged("Problems")` on the shell**. Diagnostics arrive through `DocumentViewModel.ApplyAnalysisResult`, which notifies on the *document*, not the shell, and `ResultsView.DataContext` is assigned once and never reassigned — so Avalonia read `Problems` exactly once at bind time (when `Diagnostics` was still `[]`) and never again. The pane would have rendered permanently empty, which also meant the `SendToEditor` wiring added specifically to make its double-click-to-navigate work could never have fired. Fixed by having `ShellViewModel` raise `Problems` at every point its derived value can change: a new `raiseProblemsChanged` helper is called alongside the three existing `ActiveDocument` raises in `OpenDocument`/`CloseDocument`, and `wireDocument` now subscribes to each document's own `PropertyChanged` and re-raises `Problems` when that document's `Diagnostics` change *and* it is currently the active tab (a background tab's analysis completing changes nothing the pane is showing). `Problems`'s doc comment, which until now claimed it was un-observable "since nothing binds to it yet", was corrected — something binds to it as of this task.

`MainWindow` now constructs the shared `NativeWebView` and the `ResultsView` itself (rather than finding them by name in XAML), because `DockFactory.CreateLayout()` needs live control instances to assign to each dockable's `Context`. Init sequence in code-behind is `CreateLayout()` → `InitLayout(layout)` → `dockControl.Factory <- …` → `dockControl.Layout <- …`; `InitializeFactory`/`InitializeLayout` are deliberately left off the XAML element so there is exactly one init path. `DockFactory.DocumentActivated` now pushes a `DocumentLoaded` envelope into the shared editor, which is the piece Task 7 built the notification for.

Real API deviation from the brief: the installed `Avalonia.Controls.WebView` 12.0.1 `NativeWebView` has **no** `PostWebMessage`/`PostWebMessageAsJson`/`PostWebMessageAsString` method — verified by reflecting over `Avalonia.Controls.WebView.dll` through a `MetadataLoadContext`, which lists exactly one outbound member, `InvokeScript(string) -> Task<string>` (plus `Navigate`/`NavigateToString`/`GoBack`/`GoForward`/`Refresh`/`Stop`/print/cookie/command helpers). Host→editor envelopes are therefore delivered by `InvokeScript`, dispatching a synthetic `message` event on `window.chrome.webview` — the exact object `editor-host.html`'s existing listener is attached to, so no HTML change was needed. The JSON payload is serialized twice (once to the envelope JSON, once more to produce a properly escaped JS string literal), and the call is guarded against both a synchronous throw (`try`/`with`) and a faulted task (an `OnlyOnFaulted` continuation), so a message sent before the page finishes loading can never take the window down. `ShellViewModel.SendToEditor` is wired to the same helper here, since the WebView is now private to `MainWindow` and this is the only place that hook can be reached — without it, the newly bound Problems tab's double-click navigation would be a permanent no-op.

Also verified before writing markup, so the build wouldn't be the first place a wrong name showed up: all seven `MaterialIconKind` values used in the toolbar (`FilePlus`, `FolderOpen`, `ContentSave`, `Play`, `Stop`, `ContentCopy`, `Restore`) exist in `Material.Icons` 3.0.2, and `Dock.Model.Mvvm.Controls.Document` really does expose `Context: obj` and nothing named `Content` (re-confirming Task 5's finding across all inherited properties, not just declared ones).

Every existing event handler was preserved: the Run/Cancel/Copy Result/Reset Session buttons keep their original control names so their `FindControl` wiring is unchanged, and Run/Cancel/Reset Session gained menu-item twins. New public members `MainWindow.ThemeService` and `MainWindow.DockFactory` are exposed for Tasks 10/12; View > Toggle Theme flips `ThemeService` and pushes the matching `ThemeVariant` onto `Application.Current`.

TDD'd the `Problems` notification fix: four tests written first (`PropertyChanged` fires for `Problems` when the active document's diagnostics change / when `OpenDocument` switches tabs / when `CloseDocument` changes which tab is active, and does *not* fire when a background document's diagnostics change). `dotnet test tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests --filter FullyQualifiedName~Problems` failed 3 of 5 first, real RED — the diagnostics case reported `Collection: []`, i.e. the shell raised nothing at all, exactly the defect. Same filtered run passed 5/5 after the fix. One pre-existing test (`PropertyChanged fires for ActiveDocument when OpenDocument switches to a different tab`) recorded only the *last* property name raised, so it was changed to collect all raised names and assert `Contains "ActiveDocument"` — same intent, no longer order-sensitive now that `Problems` is raised alongside `ActiveDocument`.

Build verification: `dotnet build` on the full solution clean, 0 warnings, 0 errors — which also proves the XAML itself compiled (Avalonia's XamlIl resolves control types, property names, and event handler methods at build time; `ResultsView.axaml`/`MainWindow.axaml` and a generated `!XamlIlPopulate` were confirmed present in the built `FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.dll`). Full `dotnet test` across all 12 test projects: 379 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (+4 over UI-Foundation.8's 375), `Ui.Tests` 96 (+4). Actual visual rendering and docking behavior are not verifiable here (no display, no headless XAML harness in this codebase) — same as every prior XAML task (A.10, A.13, A.16, B.6, B.9, E.6, E.7) — and are deferred to Task 14's manual acceptance pass. In particular, "the Problems pane now updates live" is proven at the view-model level by the tests above, not at the rendering level; Task 14 should still confirm rows actually appear after a run.

## 2026-08-06 — UI-Foundation.10: File New/Open/Save/Save As

Wired the six dead controls Task 9 left unhandled (`NewMenuItem`/`OpenMenuItem`/`SaveMenuItem`/`SaveAsMenuItem`/`ExitMenuItem` and the `NewButton`/`OpenButton`/`SaveButton` toolbar twins) in `MainWindow.axaml.fs`, using `this.StorageProvider` (Avalonia 12's `IStorageProvider`, reachable directly off `Window`) and `DockFactory.OpenDocument`. New creates a fresh `untitled:ScriptN.fsx` tab. Open shows an `.fsx`-filtered file picker, reads the chosen file's real bytes via plain `System.IO.File`, and loads them into the new tab. Save writes straight to `doc.Uri` for a file-backed tab, or falls through to the same save-picker as Save As when the active tab is still `untitled:`.

Two real deviations from the brief's assumed shape, both mechanical once the compiler flagged them:
- `IStorageProvider.SaveFilePickerAsync` returns `Task<IStorageFile>` (a nullable reference), not `Task<IStorageFile option>` as the brief's pattern match assumed — swapped `match file with None/Some` for `if not (isNull file)`.
- `this.VisualRoot :?> Window` doesn't compile from inside a lambda (`Visual.VisualRoot` is `protected`, inaccessible outside the declaring type even from a closure defined in it) — moot anyway, since `MainWindow` already *is* a `Window`, so `ExitMenuItem` just calls `this.Close()` directly.

Also fixed a real sequencing bug in the brief's own `openDocument`, not just an API mismatch: its original order calls `dockFactory.OpenDocument(path)` *before* `doc.ApplyTextChanged(...)`. `dockFactory.OpenDocument` synchronously activates the new tab, which fires `DocumentActivated` (Task 9's wiring) and pushes a `DocumentLoaded` bridge notification carrying the document's `Text`/`Version` *at that instant* — still empty, since the freshly-created `DocumentViewModel` starts blank and `ApplyTextChanged` hadn't run yet. The shared Monaco editor would have rendered an empty buffer for every opened file even though the view model held the real text internally. Fixed by populating the document first — `shellViewModel.OpenDocument(path)` (the same shell-only half `DockFactory.OpenDocument` calls internally) creates/returns the `DocumentViewModel`, `ApplyTextChanged` loads the real text into it, and only then does `dockFactory.OpenDocument(path)` create the Dock tab and activate it, so the `DocumentLoaded` notification carries the actual file content.

Known gap, not fixed here (out of this task's scope — would need a new `DocumentViewModel` API, and neither the brief nor the plan's Task 14 acceptance checklist asks for it): saving an `untitled:` tab through the picker writes the file but doesn't rename the tab's `Uri` to the saved path or clear `IsDirty` (no such setter exists on `DocumentViewModel` yet). A second Save on the same tab will prompt again instead of writing straight through. Worth revisiting if Task 14's manual pass finds it surprising.

## 2026-08-07 — UI-Foundation.11: View menu tool-window visibility toggles

Wired the two `MenuItem`s Task 9 left dead (`ToggleWorkspaceMenuItem`/`ToggleResultsMenuItem`) to actually hide and re-show the Workspace and Results tool panes. This task's brief deliberately left the hide/show mechanism unresearched — unlike every prior task, it gave a placeholder instead of working code — so the real `Dock.Model.Core.IFactory` API had to be found before writing anything.

Reflecting over the installed `Dock.Model.dll` (v12.1.0, from `src/FSharpQueryStudio.App/bin/Debug/net10.0/`) showed `IFactory` exposes `HideDockable(IDockable)`/`HideDockable(string id)` and `RestoreDockable(IDockable)`/`RestoreDockable(string id)`, with matching `DockableHidden`/`DockableRestored` events — and `IDockable` has no simpler `IsVisible`-style flag at all (dumped its full property list; nothing fits). Rather than trust the string-`id` overloads by name, a throwaway xunit test (`DockFactoryTests.fs`, run via `dotnet test --filter FullyQualifiedName~SCRATCH` before being replaced with real assertions) exercised them against a live `DockFactory`: `HideDockable(tool)` removes the tool from its `ToolDock.VisibleDockables` as expected (`Count` 1→0, `tool.Owner` flips from the `ToolDock` to the root dock), but `RestoreDockable("Results")` — a fresh lookup by id *after* the hide — left the pane hidden (`Count` stayed 0). `RestoreDockable` only works when handed the *exact* `IDockable` reference that was hidden, presumably because a hidden dockable is no longer reachable via the normal tree traversal `Find`/id-lookup uses. Re-running with the retained reference (`RestoreDockable(resultsTool)`) round-tripped correctly (`Count` 0→1, `Owner` back to the `ToolDock`).

That finding drove the implementation: `DockFactory` gained four new exposing members (`WorkspaceDock`/`ResultsDock`, the `IToolDock`s, and `WorkspaceTool`/`ResultsTool`, the `IDockable`s themselves) so `MainWindow` can hold onto the same `Tool` instances for the lifetime of the window rather than re-deriving them each click — re-deriving from `VisibleDockables` would fail the moment the pane is hidden, since that's exactly the collection the tool drops out of. `MainWindow.axaml.fs`'s `toggleToolVisibility` checks current visibility by reference-equality against the owning dock's `VisibleDockables` and calls `HideDockable`/`RestoreDockable` accordingly — a real toggle, not a one-way hide.

TDD'd at the `DockFactory` level: two new tests (`DockFactoryTests.fs`) assert a full hide→restore round-trip for both the Results and Workspace tools, checking `VisibleDockables` membership before hide, after hide, and after restore.

Actual visual show/hide behavior isn't verifiable in this environment (no display) — deferred to Task 14's manual acceptance pass, same as every prior docking/rendering task; the `DockFactory`-level round-trip tests are the strongest verification available here.

## 2026-08-07 — UI-Foundation.12: layout + theme persistence wiring

Wired `Storage.UiState.read`/`.write` into `App.axaml.fs` so the dock layout and theme choice survive a restart. Like Task 11, this task's brief left a placeholder (`<SerializerTypeFromTask3>`) instead of a real class name, so the installed `Dock.Serializer.SystemTextJson` package (v12.1.0, reflected from `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/Dock.Serializer.SystemTextJson.dll`) had to be inspected first: it exposes one public class, `Dock.Serializer.SystemTextJson.DockSerializer`, implementing `Dock.Model.Core.IDockSerializer` with a parameterless constructor and generic `Serialize<T>(T) : string` / `Deserialize<T>(string) : T` methods — exactly the shape the brief's placeholder code already assumed, so the sample code needed no restructuring, just the real name dropped in.

`MainWindow.axaml.fs` changed shape across Tasks 9-11, so its current state (not the plan's original prose) was read in full before touching it: `dockControl` was a local `let` inside the constructor's `do` block, invisible outside it. Hoisted it to a `let mutable dockControl: DockControl = Unchecked.defaultof<_>` field (assigned in the `do` block, same pattern as `assetHost`), then added two new members: `MainDockLayout` (reads `dockControl.Layout`, an `IDock`) and `RestoreLayout(root: IRootDock)` (assigns `dockControl.Layout <- root`) — both narrow, single-purpose surfaces rather than exposing `dockControl` itself, matching how `DockFactory`/`ThemeService` are already exposed.

`App.axaml.fs` now: loads `UiState` from `%LOCALAPPDATA%/FSharpQueryStudio` before constructing `MainWindow`, applies the saved theme via `ThemeService.Toggle()` (mirroring Task 1's single-mutator design) when it differs from the default Dark, restores a saved `DockLayout` after `MainWindow`'s own default layout is built (`DockSerializer().Deserialize<IRootDock>` → `dockFactory.InitLayout` → `mainWindow.RestoreLayout`, all inside a `try`/`with` that logs and falls through to the untouched default layout on any failure — a missing, corrupt, or version-incompatible `ui-state.json` must never block startup), and saves both theme and layout on `mainWindow.Closing` (layout serialization wrapped in its own `try`/`with`, downgrading to `DockLayout = None` on failure rather than losing the theme write too). `FSharpQueryStudio.App.fsproj` needed a new `ProjectReference` to `FSharpQueryStudio.Storage` (previously unreferenced by `App`); `Dock.Serializer.SystemTextJson` itself needed no new package reference — `FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.fsproj` already pulls it in as a transitive dependency from Task 3.

## 2026-08-07 — Fix: UI-Foundation.12's `DockSerializer.Serialize` threw on every real layout

Code review (real reflection/repro, not assumed) found the composition-root wiring above was correct but `DockSerializer().Serialize(mainWindow.MainDockLayout)` threw `JsonException: A possible object cycle was detected` on every real layout — the `try`/`with` around it caught it silently, so `DockLayout` was written as `null` on every close and the whole feature was dead code despite the green build/tests (nothing in the suite exercised real serialization against a real layout).

Root cause, confirmed by reflecting over the installed package's real source (GitHub) and reflecting over the built `Dock.Model.Mvvm.dll`: `IDockable.Owner` (the back-reference to a dockable's parent) is a plain `[DataMember]`, not `[IgnoreDataMember]` — every non-trivial layout has a real cycle through it (child → `Owner` → parent → `VisibleDockables` → child). `DockSerializer`'s options do set `ReferenceHandler.Preserve`, but its own `JsonConverterList<T>.Write` serializes each list element via a *nested* `JsonSerializer.Serialize(writer, item, options)` call, and each nested call starts its own independent reference-tracking scope — so `Preserve`'s cycle protection never spans a list boundary, and every real tree (which is nothing but list boundaries) blows past `MaxDepth` 64 and throws. Confirmed via a throwaway `dotnet fsi` script that built a real `DockFactory` layout exactly the way `MainWindow`'s constructor does and called `DockSerializer().Serialize<IDock>` on it directly — reproduced the exact exception every run, no newer package version exists on NuGet, and the alternate `Save<T>(Stream, T)` method threw identically (same code path).

Fix, in a new `LayoutSerializer` module (`src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/LayoutSerializer.fs`): `Owner` is fully redundant on the wire (`IFactory.InitLayout` already reconstructs it after every deserialize, the same call `MainWindow`'s constructor makes on a freshly-built layout), so it's stripped from the JSON via a `JsonTypeInfo` modifier attached to the package's own polymorphic type resolver (`DockModelPolymorphicTypeResolver` — `internal` to the package, but derives from the public `DefaultJsonTypeInfoResolver`, so an instance obtained via `Activator.CreateInstance` still exposes the public `Modifiers` list needed, without having to reimplement the resolver's `$type`-discriminator handling from scratch).

That fix alone wasn't sufficient: with `Owner` stripped, `Serialize` succeeded structurally but then failed inside `ScriptDocument.Document` (an `FSharpQueryStudio.Protocol.SessionMode` F# DU, which plain `System.Text.Json` can't serialize) — and even after excluding that property too, `Deserialize` failed separately with `InvalidOperationException: ... constructor ... must bind to an object property or field`, because `ScriptDocument` has only a 3-argument constructor (`document`/`shell`/`editorContent` — none of which exist as plain JSON data) and can never be reconstructed by System.Text.Json's default object-converter, regardless of what properties are included or excluded. This isn't a bug to route around — a document tab's content was never going to survive a restart anyway, same reason `ScriptDocument.Context` (the shared WebView) is already `[IgnoreDataMember]`. So `DockFactory` gained two new members: `SerializeLayout` (temporarily empties `documentDock`'s tab content for the one `Serialize` call, then restores it immediately — the live, still-open tabs are completely unaffected) and `RestoreDocumentTabs` (after a successful deserialize, finds the now-empty `documentDock` placeholder in the restored tree by `Id` — a plain `[DataMember]` string that does round-trip — and splices the live `documentDock` back in place of it, so the restored layout shows the real open tab(s) instead of a blank document pane). `App.axaml.fs` calls both: `SerializeLayout` on `Closing`, and `RestoreDocumentTabs` (followed by a second `InitLayout` to re-wire `Owner`/`Factory` on the grafted node) right after a successful `Deserialize` at startup.

All of this was verified against a *real* `DockFactory` layout via throwaway `dotnet fsi` scripts before being turned into production code and a real xunit regression test — not assumed to work from reading the stack trace. Added `LayoutSerializerTests.fs` to `Ui.Tests` (3 tests, same "build a real `DockFactory` layout" pattern `DockFactoryTests.fs` already uses): `Serialize`-then-`Deserialize` doesn't throw; a `Proportion` change on a tool pane (`ResultsDock`/`WorkspaceDock`) survives the round-trip; `RestoreDocumentTabs` grafts the live `DocumentDock` back and the restored document tab is the same `ScriptDocument` with the same `Document.Uri`.

Build verification: `dotnet build` on the full solution clean, 0 warnings, 0 errors. Full `dotnet test`: 384 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (+3 over the first UI-Foundation.12 pass, all in `Ui.Tests`, now 101) — including the 3 new `LayoutSerializerTests`. Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/LayoutSerializer.fs` (new), `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/DockFactory.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.App/App.axaml.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.fsproj`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests.fsproj`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/LayoutSerializerTests.fs` (new). Actual restart-and-restore behavior is still not verifiable in this environment (no display) — deferred to Task 14's manual acceptance pass, as before, but the underlying `Serialize`/`Deserialize` round-trip that behavior depends on is now real, tested, and confirmed not to throw, rather than untested code that only appeared to work.

## 2026-08-07 — Fix: UI-Foundation.12's restore path orphaned tool-pane content and left a duplicate ActiveDockable branch

A second code-review pass (the Owner-cycle fix above was confirmed correct and reproduced independently) activated the restore path for the first time — it had been dead code until the cycle fix, since `DockLayout` had always been written as `null` — and activating it surfaced three more real bugs in the same area, two Critical/Important:

1. **Critical — the restore path orphaned the live Workspace/Results tool controls.** `RestoreDocumentTabs` only handled the document tab; nothing re-attached `resultsTool`'s live `Context` (the real `ResultsView`, `[IgnoreDataMember]` so it always deserializes as `null`) or repointed `DockFactory`'s cached `workspaceDock`/`resultsDock`/`workspaceTool`/`resultsTool` fields at the restored tree's instances — they'd keep pointing at pre-restore objects no longer actually in the tree, making Task 11's View-menu visibility toggles silent no-ops post-restore.
2. **Important — the restored root's `ActiveDockable` was a duplicate branch that didn't contain the grafted document dock.** Traced to the same nested-`Serialize`-per-list-boundary defect behind the original cycle bug: `VisibleDockables.[0]` gets written as one `$id`-tagged object, but `ActiveDockable`/`DefaultDockable`/`FocusedDockable` get written as a second, full duplicate copy rather than a `$ref`. Confirmed empirically (`root.ActiveDockable === root.VisibleDockables.[0]` is `false` after a real round-trip, at every `IDock` level in the tree, not just the root) via a throwaway `dotnet fsi` script before writing any fix.
3. **Important, easy fix — `LayoutSerializer.create()` was called outside both `try`/`with` guards** in `App.axaml.fs`. A future package upgrade renaming the internal type it reflects into would have propagated an unhandled exception straight out of `OnFrameworkInitializationCompleted`, failing the app to launch at all — not the graceful degradation the rest of the feature promises.

Fixes: `LayoutSerializer.normalize` (new) repairs the `ActiveDockable`/`DefaultDockable`/`FocusedDockable` duplication by walking the tree and, per `IDock`, replacing any of the three with the matching entry from that dock's own `VisibleDockables` — matched *locally* per container (by `Id` when present among the children, falling back to "the only child" when there's exactly one), not via a single tree-wide `Id`-keyed map, because several containers in this layout (the `ProportionalDock` rows/columns) never get an `Id` assigned at all and a global map would silently collide different anonymous nodes onto the same key (confirmed by testing the naive global-map version first — it failed to fix anything). `App.axaml.fs` calls it right after `Deserialize`, before `InitLayout`. `DockFactory` gained `RestoreToolPanes` (finds the restored `WorkspacePane`/`ResultsPane`/`Workspace`/`Results` nodes by `Id`, repairs `resultsTool.Context`, and repoints the cached fields at the restored instances — deliberately *not* a wholesale graft like the document dock, since the restored `ToolDock` nodes correctly carry the persisted `Proportion`/`Alignment` and overwriting them would throw that away) and both it and `RestoreDocumentTabs`'s return values are now checked in `App.axaml.fs`: either failing falls back to the default layout, the same as any other restore failure, instead of showing a broken partial restore. `RestoreDocumentTabs` also now carries the placeholder's restored `Proportion` onto the live `documentDock` before grafting it in, so the earlier fix's wholesale document-dock replacement doesn't silently discard the document pane's last-saved size — a latent bug noticed while fixing the reviewer's findings, not something explicitly flagged, but directly relevant to the same "does the graft preserve restored geometry" question. `layoutSerializer` in `App.axaml.fs` is now built inside its own `try`/`with`, `DockSerializer option` instead of `DockSerializer`, threaded through both call sites.

Verified the whole normalize/graft sequence end to end against a real `DockFactory` layout via `dotnet fsi` before writing production code (same discipline as the first fix): confirmed the duplication, confirmed `normalize` repairs it (including at a nested `ToolDock`, not just root), confirmed `RestoreToolPanes` repoints `Context` and the cached fields, confirmed the document-dock graft still works with `Proportion` carried over. Added 4 more tests to `LayoutSerializerTests.fs`: `normalize` repairs the duplication (root and nested); `RestoreToolPanes` repairs `Context` and repoints the cached fields (proven by reference identity, not just by value — a value-only check wouldn't distinguish "repointed" from "coincidentally already correct" for a `ToolDock` whose own `Proportion` is never touched by `SerializeLayout`); `RestoreToolPanes` returns `false` without mutating state when a pane `Id` is missing; and a full-sequence test confirming `root.ActiveDockable`/the restored `DocumentDock`'s own `ActiveDockable` are reference-equal to their `VisibleDockables.[0]` and the Results tool's `Context` is the live object, end to end.

Build verification: `dotnet build` on the full solution clean, 0 warnings, 0 errors. Full `dotnet test`: 388 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (+4 over the previous fix, all in `Ui.Tests`, now 105). Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/LayoutSerializer.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/DockFactory.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.App/App.axaml.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/LayoutSerializerTests.fs`. Actual restart-and-restore behavior remains deferred to Task 14's manual pass (no display in this environment) — but the tool-pane content, `ActiveDockable` reachability, and document-tab identity that behavior depends on are now all demonstrated real via a real `DockFactory` layout, not merely assumed.

## 2026-08-07 — Fix: UI-Foundation.12's restore path ignored hidden panes and could repoint state for a rejected restore

A third code-review pass (driving the round-2 fix through more realistic scenarios with the same `dotnet fsi` harness that's caught every real bug in this task) independently reproduced and confirmed all three round-2 fixes work — then found two more real bugs in the same area:

1. **Important — hiding a tool pane before closing silently disabled ALL layout persistence on every subsequent launch.** `IFactory.HideDockable` (Task 11's View-menu toggle) moves a tool out of `VisibleDockables` into `IRootDock.HiddenDockables`, and that list round-trips through JSON correctly — but `DockFactory.FindById` only searched `VisibleDockables`, so a hidden tool could never be found on restore. Concretely: hide the Workspace pane, close, relaunch — `RestoreToolPanes` (via `FindById`) returns `false`, the whole restore falls back to the default layout, discarding every persisted proportion, not just the hidden pane's own state — and it stays broken on every future launch until the user un-hides the pane and closes again.
2. **Important — the partial-graft fallback could repoint `DockFactory`'s cached fields even when the restore was about to be rejected and discarded.** `RestoreDocumentTabs`/`RestoreToolPanes` ran unconditionally, before the caller decided whether to actually use the restored tree — and `RestoreToolPanes` repoints `DockFactory`'s cached `WorkspaceDock`/`ResultsDock`/`WorkspaceTool`/`ResultsTool` fields the moment it finds a match. So on a saved layout missing just one pane's `Id` (an old/hand-edited `ui-state.json` — exactly the scenario the fallback exists for), the app correctly kept showing the default layout on screen, but `DockFactory`'s cached fields ended up pointing into the discarded/orphaned restored tree instead — reproducing round 2's "View-menu toggle is a silent no-op" symptom via a different path. Reproduced directly via `dotnet fsi`: built a real layout, hand-corrupted the saved JSON's `"Documents"` id, confirmed the old unconditional-call order really did leave `factory.ResultsDock` pointing at the (about to be discarded) restored tree.

Fixes: `DockFactory.FindById` (renamed its search helper to `ChildLists`, shared by every restore-time graft) now also searches `HiddenDockables` and the four `*PinnedDockables` lists on `IRootDock`, not just `VisibleDockables`. Added `DockFactory.CanRestore` — a pure dry-run check (no mutation) for whether the document dock and both tool panes/tools are all locatable in a restored tree — and `App.axaml.fs` now calls it BEFORE calling either mutating `RestoreDocumentTabs`/`RestoreToolPanes`, so a restore that's going to be rejected never mutates `DockFactory`'s live state at all. `LayoutSerializer.normalize` picked up the same `Hidden`/`Pinned`-list traversal for consistency, plus a defensive null-guard on `VisibleDockables` (reachable only via a hand-truncated `ui-state.json`; already caught by `App.axaml.fs`'s outer `try`/`with`, but `normalize` shouldn't rely solely on that).

Verified via `dotnet fsi` against a real `DockFactory` layout before writing production code: hid the Workspace tool, confirmed the saved JSON really contains it (via `HiddenDockables`), confirmed `CanRestore` now finds it (`true`) and both grafts succeed; separately, hand-corrupted a saved layout's `"Documents"` id, confirmed `CanRestore` correctly returns `false` and — critically — confirmed NO mutation of `DockFactory`'s cached fields occurs in that case, versus a side-by-side run proving the OLD unconditional-call order really did corrupt `factory.ResultsDock`. Added 2 more tests to `LayoutSerializerTests.fs`: a hidden tool pane round-trips through `CanRestore`/`RestoreToolPanes`; `CanRestore` returning `false` leaves `DockFactory`'s cached fields and `documentDock.Proportion` completely untouched.

Reflected on `IFactory`'s full member list while investigating a possible "fourth interaction" (asked for explicitly, not to fix speculatively): confirmed `PinDockable`/`FloatDockable`/`AddWindow`/`IRootDock.Windows` are all real, fully-supported operations in the installed package — genuine `Dock.Avalonia` gestures a real `DockControl`'s built-in drag-and-drop likely exposes regardless of what this app's own menus wire up (Task 11 only wired Hide/Show, never Pin or float-to-window). `IRootDock.Windows`/`IDockWindow` (a floating/torn-off window's own nested dock tree) is not walked by `FindById`/`normalize` at all - if a user ever tears a tab into a floating window and closes while it's floating, anything only reachable through `Windows` wouldn't be found, and thanks to this fix's `CanRestore` gate that now fails *safe* (falls back to the default layout) rather than corrupting state, but the arrangement wouldn't restore. Pinned dockables are included in the traversal defensively but were never exercised against a real `PinDockable` call the way `HideDockable` was for this fix. Flagging both, not fixing either now — Task 14 or a future task should decide whether floating-window/pin support is in scope.

Build verification: `dotnet build` on the full solution clean, 0 warnings, 0 errors. Full `dotnet test`: 390 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (+2 over the previous fix, all in `Ui.Tests`, now 107). Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/DockFactory.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/LayoutSerializer.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.App/App.axaml.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/LayoutSerializerTests.fs`.

## 2026-08-07 — Fix: restoring a hidden tool pane left `OriginalOwner` unset, making the un-hide toggle destructive

A fourth code-review pass confirmed round 3's fix works — a tool pane hidden at save time really does come back hidden after a relaunch instead of the whole restore being rejected — but found that the restored pane could then never be un-hidden again. `IDockable.OriginalOwner` is not part of the serialized wire format, and nothing in `RestoreToolPanes` re-established it, so a restored hidden tool came back with `OriginalOwner = null`. `IFactory.RestoreDockable` (the un-hide half of Task 11's View-menu toggle) reads `OriginalOwner` to know which `ToolDock` to put a hidden dockable back into: with it null, it removed the tool from `IRootDock.HiddenDockables` and put it nowhere at all. The pane never reappeared, stayed unreachable from anywhere in the tree for the rest of the session, and the next save then wrote a layout missing that tool entirely — which the launch after that rejected wholesale via `CanRestore`, discarding all persisted layout state.

Reproduced against the real installed Dock assemblies and the real compiled `DockFactory` via `dotnet fsi` before writing any fix (hide Workspace → save → restore → click View > Workspace twice): `restored tool OriginalOwner is null = true`, `tool now visible in WorkspaceDock = false`, `root.HiddenDockables.Count = 0` (removed from Hidden, put nowhere), `Workspace tool still reachable anywhere in tree = false`. The same script with `OriginalOwner` repaired after restore reported `true`/`0`/`true` — the un-hide works.

Fix: `DockFactory.RestoreToolPanes` now sets each restored tool's `OriginalOwner` to its own restored `ToolDock`, alongside the existing `Context` repair and cached-field repointing. Set unconditionally, which is safe — for a pane that was *visible* at save time, `HideDockable` overwrites `OriginalOwner` on the next hide anyway, and setting it means a pane the user hides later in the session is equally un-hideable. Also tightened `RestoreDocumentTabs`'s `replace` walk with the same `VisibleDockables` null-guard `ChildLists`/`LayoutSerializer.normalize` already carry (reachable only via a hand-truncated `ui-state.json`; already caught by `App.axaml.fs`'s outer `try`/`with`, but this walk shouldn't be the one place relying on that outer net).

Verified after the fix by re-running the same `fsi` reproduction against the newly built `DockFactory` — the un-hide now works with no manual repair — plus a three-generation probe (hide → save → restore → un-hide → save again → restore again) confirming `CanRestore` stays `true` throughout, the gen-2 JSON still contains the tool, and the pane comes back *visible* on the third launch and can still be re-hidden. Added 1 test to `LayoutSerializerTests.fs` that drives the real un-hide path end to end (mirroring `MainWindow`'s `toggleToolVisibility` exactly, `IFactory.RestoreDockable` and all) and asserts the tool is genuinely back in its `ToolDock`'s `VisibleDockables` and out of `HiddenDockables` — not merely that `CanRestore`/`RestoreToolPanes` returned `true`, which is the gap round 3's own test had.

Build verification: `dotnet build` on the full solution clean, 0 warnings, 0 errors. Full `dotnet test`: 391 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (+1 over the previous fix, in `Ui.Tests`, now 108). Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/DockFactory.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/LayoutSerializerTests.fs`.

## 2026-08-07 — UI-Foundation.14: Milestone acceptance pass

UI-Foundation replaced the Milestone A-E fixed-`Grid` Avalonia shell with a real `Dock.Avalonia`-based dockable, VS2026-styled shell: a `DockControl`-hosted layout (Workspace/Documents/Results tool windows, `DockFactory`-built), a dark/light VS2026 theme pair with a runtime toggle, File New/Open/Save wired to a real `IStorageProvider`, View-menu tool-pane hide/show, and layout+theme persistence across restarts. Thirteen tasks, each independently TDD'd and reviewed. (UI-Foundation.13, the `docs/fs-studio-plan.md` Section 5 update, is the one task in this plan without its own dated entry above — its work is folded into this milestone summary instead.)

**Full solution:** `dotnet build` clean across all 24 projects (12 `src` + 12 `tests`), 0 warnings, 0 errors. `dotnet test` across all 12 test projects: 391 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (`Security.Tests` 10, `Protocol.Tests` 10, `Logging.Tests` 11, `Storage.Tests` 29, `Editor.Tests` 24, `Ui.Tests` 108, `Analysis.Tests` 42, `Connectors.Tests` 39, `Results.Tests` 20, `Execution.Tests` 28, `Worker.Tests` 24, `Integration.Tests` 46) — matches UI-Foundation.12's fourth-round-fix count exactly, confirming nothing regressed between then and this task.

**Real launch verification, no display available (same limitation and same handling as A.10/A.13/A.16/B.6/B.9/E.6/E.7):** ran the built `FSharpQueryStudio.App.exe` directly (not `dotnet run`, per A.13's finding that the shared `dotnet.exe` host doesn't carry the embedded manifest) three times.

1. Cold launch, killed after a 10s bounded window: logged `F# Query Studio host starting` then `Host window ready` ~2.4s later, ran the full window with no exception anywhere in the log, was still alive at kill time (not a crash-on-boot), and left no orphaned `FSharpQueryStudio.App`/`msedgewebview2` process behind.
2. Launch, then a **graceful** close via `Process.CloseMainWindow()` (not a forced kill, so `MainWindow.Closing` actually fires) after 4s: the process exited cleanly on its own within 8s, and `%LOCALAPPDATA%\FSharpQueryStudio\ui-state.json` was written with `"theme":"Dark"` and a real, non-null `dockLayout` blob (~24.7KB of real Dock.Avalonia JSON, not the round-1 `null` bug from UI-Foundation.12's first fix) — concrete proof the `Serialize`/`Owner`-cycle fix holds against a real live layout, not just the `dotnet fsi` repro scripts cited in the Task 12 fix entries above.
3. Relaunch on top of that just-written `ui-state.json`, killed after 6s: reached `Host window ready` in ~1.4s with **no** `Failed to restore saved dock layout`, `Saved dock layout was missing an expected pane`, or `...graft still failed` warning in the log — the three distinct log lines `App.axaml.fs` emits on every restore-path failure branch. Their absence, plus no exception and a normal `Host window ready`, is real evidence the `Deserialize` → `normalize` → `CanRestore` → `RestoreDocumentTabs`/`RestoreToolPanes` → `InitLayout` → `RestoreLayout` sequence ran to completion on a real saved layout, not merely that the app didn't crash. (Those three are the logged failure branches, not an exhaustive list of every way this could silently no-op — `Deserialize` returning `null` without throwing, at `App.axaml.fs` around line 128, falls through with no log line at all. Not a live concern here: the round-trip evidence above is a real non-null `dockLayout` being written and a fast ~1.4s ready with no warnings, not an absence-of-evidence argument alone.)

This proves the persistence *mechanism* engages successfully end to end for the first time outside a `dotnet fsi` scratch script. It does **not** prove the restored layout renders correctly on screen — that remains genuinely unverifiable without a display, same as every item below.

**Manual verification checklist** (from the Task 14 brief), item by item:

- Dark VS2026 palette by default — **deferred to user.** `RequestedThemeVariant <- "Dark"` and `VsDark.axaml`'s resources are confirmed to build/compile clean (UI-Foundation.8/.9 build verification), but actual rendered appearance can't be seen here.
- `View > Toggle Theme` switches and back — **deferred to user.** Code path exists (`ToggleThemeMenuItem.Click` flips `ThemeService` and pushes the matching `ThemeVariant`, confirmed by reading `MainWindow.axaml.fs` directly this task), but no automated or log-based signal distinguishes a rendered light vs. dark window.
- Workspace/Documents/Results all visible in the default layout — **partially confirmed programmatically.** `DockFactoryTests.fs` (UI-Foundation.6) builds this exact layout and asserts all three panes exist in the tree with the right `VisibleDockables` membership; this task's own launch (above) reached `Host window ready` against that same default layout with no exception. Whether they're actually *visible on screen* is deferred to the user.
- Float/re-dock the Results pane without a crash — **deferred to user.** This is `Dock.Avalonia`'s own built-in drag-and-drop, never exercised by any automated test in this plan. Worth flagging what UI-Foundation.12's fourth review round already noted: `IRootDock.Windows` (a torn-off floating window's own nested dock tree) is not walked by `FindById`/`normalize`, so a tab left floating at close time may not survive a restart-restore even though it won't crash the float/re-dock gesture itself.
- `File > New` opens a second tab without affecting the first — **partially confirmed programmatically.** `ShellViewModel.OpenDocument`/`DockFactory.OpenDocument` create a distinct `DocumentViewModel`/`ScriptDocument` pair per URI (`ShellViewModelTests.fs`, `DockFactoryTests.fs`), so the two tabs' underlying state is provably independent. Whether typing in the second tab's on-screen Monaco editor leaves the first tab's rendered text untouched is deferred to the user.
- Switching tabs shows each tab's own text (`DocumentLoaded`/shared-WebView bridge) — **deferred to user; the single highest-risk item on this list.** UI-Foundation.7 only built and tested the notification's encode/JS-handling half (`EditorBridgeTests.fs`, one test); UI-Foundation.9 wired `DockFactory.DocumentActivated` to actually send it via `MainWindow`'s `InvokeScript`-based `SendToEditor`. No automated test exercises the real Monaco `setValue` call this depends on, and the brief itself flagged this as the behavior "most likely to need a real fix if Monaco's `setValue` timing doesn't match what Task 7's code assumed." Still true after this task — it needs a real interactive check.
- `File > Open` loads real `.fsx` contents — **deferred to user.** `OpenFilePickerAsync` showing a real OS picker isn't reachable here (no display); UI-Foundation.10's own fix (loading text via `ApplyTextChanged` *before* activating the Dock tab, so `DocumentLoaded` carries real content instead of an empty buffer) is unit-level plausible but not re-provable without the picker.
- `File > Save` prompts for `untitled:` tabs, saves directly for file-backed ones — **deferred to user, with a known pre-existing gap to watch for.** UI-Foundation.10's own entry documents that saving an `untitled:` tab through the picker writes the file but never renames the tab's `Uri` or clears `IsDirty` — a second Save on the same tab will prompt again instead of writing straight through. Not a regression from this task; carried forward as-is per the brief's own instruction to note it rather than treat it as new.
- Run via toolbar button, `Run` menu item, and F5 — **partially confirmed, one real gap found.** Read `MainWindow.axaml.fs` directly this task: both `RunButton.Click` and `RunMenuItem.Click` call `shellViewModel.RunAsync()` — confirmed identical wiring, not assumed. F5 is **not actually bound** anywhere — grepped the whole `Ui` project for `KeyBinding`/`HotKey`/`InputGesture`/`F5` and found only the menu label text (`"_Run (F5)"`) and a button tooltip; no `KeyBinding` element or `HotKey` property exists. The brief itself hedged this item with "F5 if already bound," so this isn't a regression this task caused — it's a real, previously-unflagged gap this task's own reading surfaced. Worth a follow-up task if F5-to-run is an actual requirement.
- Status bar worker-state/session-mode text updates on run/cancel/tab-switch — **partially confirmed programmatically.** `WorkerStateViewModel`/`DocumentViewModel.SessionStateDisplay` both raise `PropertyChanged` (Milestone A/E work, `WorkerStateViewModel` tests + `DocumentViewModel` tests), and `MainWindow.axaml`'s status bar binds directly to `WorkerState.Current`/`ActiveDocument.SessionStateDisplay`. The binding wiring is provably correct at the XAML-compiles level; actually watching the text change on screen is deferred to the user.
- Closing a dirty tab prompts before discarding — **confirmed as a genuine pre-existing gap, not a regression — but not for the reason first written here.** `ShellViewModel.ConfirmDiscardDirtyDocument` (`ShellViewModel.fs:77`) is a real, deliberately-designed extension point — `(DocumentViewModel -> bool) option`, doc-commented as "set by the composition root to show a real confirmation dialog" — and it's exercised by four real tests (`ShellViewModelTests.fs:346,362,377`, `ScriptDocumentTests.fs:51`). What's actually missing: grepped `MainWindow.axaml.fs`/`App.axaml.fs` for `Discard`/`Dirty`/`MessageBox`/`ContentDialog` and found zero hits — the composition root never assigns a real handler, so `ConfirmDiscardDirtyDocument` stays `None` in the running app. Per `ShellViewModel.CloseDocument`'s own logic (`None -> false` at line 108), closing a dirty tab today doesn't prompt *or* discard — it silently no-ops, leaving the tab open with no dialog and no feedback. Per the brief's own instruction, this is noted as a pre-existing gap rather than something Task 14 should fix.
- Relaunching restores the previous session's layout and theme — **the persistence *mechanism* is now confirmed programmatically for the first time outside a scratch script** (see the three-launch sequence above: a real graceful close wrote a real non-null layout, and a real relaunch consumed it with none of the three failure-branch warnings firing). Whether the *rendered* window actually shows the same pane sizes/visibility/theme after restore is still deferred to the user — the log evidence proves the round-trip completed without error, not what it looks like.

**Real bugs this plan's review process caught and fixed**, worth keeping as engineering history rather than glossing over: UI-Foundation.9's first cut shipped a `ShellViewModel.Problems` binding that would have rendered permanently empty (nothing raised `PropertyChanged("Problems")` on the shell, since diagnostics arrive via the per-document `PropertyChanged`, not the shell's) — caught by review, fixed with 4 new tests before merge. UI-Foundation.12's persistence feature went through four real review-and-fix rounds: round 1 found `DockSerializer.Serialize` threw on every real layout (a genuine `Dock.Serializer.SystemTextJson` v12.1.0 defect — nested per-list-boundary `Serialize` calls each starting their own reference-tracking scope, silently caught by the outer `try`/`with` so the whole feature was dead code behind green tests); round 2 found the restore path orphaned the live Workspace/Results tool controls and left `ActiveDockable` pointing at a duplicate, unreachable branch; round 3 found hiding a tool pane before closing silently disabled layout persistence on every subsequent launch, and that a rejected restore could still corrupt `DockFactory`'s live cached state; round 4 found a restored *hidden* pane's `OriginalOwner` was never re-established, making the un-hide toggle destructive. Each was reproduced for real against a live `DockFactory` layout via throwaway `dotnet fsi` scripts before any fix was written, not diagnosed from a stack trace alone. Separately, UI-Foundation.13 (the Section 5 doc update) had an unauthorized scope-creep edit corrected during its own review.

**Three sub-projects from the original UI-Foundation spec's decomposition remain explicitly out of scope and unstarted:** UI-Workspace (real workspace-explorer content — Task 9 shipped only an empty placeholder pane), UI-Connections (a Connections UI — the spec's Non-goals list this explicitly, and no task in this plan touched it), and UI-Trust-Packages. None of the thirteen implementation tasks claimed to cover them, and this acceptance pass doesn't either — they're tracked as separate future work, not a gap in UI-Foundation's own closure.

## 2026-08-10 — Final-review fix wave: three Task 14 gaps closed, plus the post-save editor re-key

The whole-branch re-review after UI-Foundation.14 turned into a real fix wave (`31dfc9c`, `7f7c3c6`, `6b0e847`, and this entry's own commit). **Three claims in the Task 14 entry above are now out of date and are corrected here rather than edited in place, so the acceptance pass stays a faithful record of what was true on 2026-08-07:**

- **"F5 is not actually bound anywhere"** — it is now. `MainWindow` adds a window-level `Window.KeyBindings` entry (`KeyGesture(Key.F5)` → a minimal internal `RelayCommand` calling `RunAsync`), landed in `7f7c3c6`. Avalonia ships no relay-command type and a `KeyBinding` can only invoke an `ICommand`, hence the small adapter. The menu item's `InputGesture` only ever *rendered* the "F5" text; the window binding is what actually fires it.
- **"Saving an `untitled:` tab never renames the tab's `Uri` or clears `IsDirty`"** — it does now, via `DocumentViewModel.MarkSaved` (`31dfc9c`), with `ScriptDocument` re-keying its `Id`/`Title` off the `Uri` change. A second Save on the same tab writes straight through instead of re-prompting. `7f7c3c6` also made every save route through a `tryWriteFile` helper that logs a warning and reports success, so `MarkSaved` only runs on a write that actually landed — a failed save can no longer clear the dirty marker and tell the user their work is safe when it isn't.
- **"`ConfirmDiscardDirtyDocument` stays `None` in the running app"** — it is wired now (`7f7c3c6`). `MainWindow` supplies a real confirmation dialog: Avalonia ships no message box, and Dock needs a synchronous `bool` back from `ScriptDocument.OnClose()`, so it's a small owned `Window` pumped on a nested `DispatcherFrame`. Cancel is the default/cancel button, and any failure to show the dialog keeps the tab — the safe answer is always the one that doesn't discard work.

The same wave also fixed: closing the last document tab leaving the document area empty while `ActiveDocument` pointed at an invisible replacement; File > New/Open tabs added straight to `VisibleDockables` keeping a null `Owner`, which made them impossible to close at all; `RouteTextChanged` routing editor `textChanged` messages by URI instead of at `ActiveDocument` (a cross-tab corruption once more than one tab could be open); `NextUntitledUri` probing for a free name instead of `Documents.Length + 1`; `UiState.read` crashing startup on a `ui-state.json` holding literal JSON `null`; `Material.Icons.Avalonia` never having its control theme registered, so all seven toolbar icons rendered blank; and a Section 5 claim that floating-window state survives a restart, which it does not.

**This entry's own fix — the interaction between two of those.** `RouteTextChanged` now matches strictly on the URI a message carries, and `MarkSaved` retags an `untitled:` document to its real path. But `editor-host.html` keeps its *own* `documentUri` variable, updated only when a `documentLoaded` message arrives, and stamps every outgoing `textChanged` with it. Nothing pushed `documentLoaded` after a save — `MainWindow` only pushed one on `DocumentActivated` (tab switch), and retagging `ScriptDocument.Id`/`Title` doesn't raise that event. So after the first save of a new file the page kept tagging keystrokes with the dead pre-save URI, `RouteTextChanged` matched nothing, and every edit was dropped in silence: File > New → type → Save → keep typing → Save again wrote the **pre-save** text, and the tab never even re-marked dirty to hint anything was lost. Fixed by routing all three save paths through a new `ShellViewModel.MarkDocumentSaved(doc, newUri)`, which wraps `MarkSaved` and pushes `DocumentLoaded(doc.Uri, doc.Version, doc.Text)` through the existing `SendToEditor` hook **when the URI actually changed and the saved document is still the active tab**. Both guards are deliberate. `documentLoaded` calls Monaco's `setValue`, which discards the editor's undo stack, so re-pushing on an ordinary same-path re-save would cost the user their undo history for nothing. And the save handlers `await` a file picker, so the user can switch tabs before the write lands — pushing then would re-key the page onto a document it isn't showing *and* overwrite the visible tab's text with the saved one's. That background case needs no push anyway: switching back raises `DockFactory.DocumentActivated`, which pushes the by-then-retagged URI itself.

Putting the wrapper on `ShellViewModel` rather than inline in `MainWindow` is what makes the bug testable at all — `MainWindow` isn't unit-testable in this codebase (it needs a real `DockControl`/WebView), but `SendToEditor` is already a composition-root hook a test can capture, the same seam `ProblemViewModel.Navigate`'s tests use.

Verification: 5 new `ShellViewModelTests` (URI-changing save pushes a well-formed `documentLoaded`; an edit typed after the save reaches the document end to end; a same-URI re-save pushes nothing; a save for a tab that's no longer active pushes nothing; no editor hook configured still marks clean and doesn't throw). Beyond the tests, a `dotnet fsi` probe stood a fake editor page next to a real `ShellViewModel` — a `pageUri` variable updated *only* on `documentLoaded`, exactly as the JS does — and ran the reported sequence both ways. Before (`doc.MarkSaved` alone): page URI stuck at `untitled:Script1.fsx`, second save wrote `"let x = 1"`, dirty flag `false`, post-save edit lost. After (`vm.MarkDocumentSaved`): page URI moved to the saved path, second save wrote `"let x = 1\nlet y = 2"`, dirty flag `true`, edit kept. The Monaco half of the round trip (`setValue` actually re-keying the live page) is still not testable here — no JS harness, no headless WebView — and stays on the user's manual list, same as every prior `editor-host.html` change.

Build/test: `dotnet build` clean across all 24 projects, 0 warnings, 0 errors. `dotnet test`: 408 passed, 12 failed, 0 skipped of 420. All 12 failures are the pre-existing Testcontainers ones in `Integration.Tests` (`SqlServerConnectorTests`, `PostgreSqlConnectorTests`, `ConnectionsRegressionTests`, `MilestoneCAcceptanceTests` — every one a `DockerUnavailableException` from `npipe://./pipe/docker_engine`, no Docker daemon in this environment), identical to the prior fix rounds and unrelated to this change. `Ui.Tests` 135, up from 130 measured on `6b0e847` immediately before this change (`git stash` + re-run, not inferred).

Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/ShellViewModel.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/MainWindow.axaml.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/DocumentViewModel.fs` (doc comment only), `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/ShellViewModelTests.fs`, `docs/DEVLOG.md`.

## 2026-08-10 — Fix: script pane rendered as an empty black rectangle, no Monaco visible

Reported directly by the user running the built app for the first time: the document pane was just the dark-theme background, no editor, no cursor, nothing to click into. Not a focus/input bug as first suspected — Monaco was never on screen to receive input at all.

**Root cause.** `ScriptDocument`/`Tool` (Task 5/6/11) correctly place the shared `NativeWebView`/`ResultsView` in `Context: obj`, since `Dock.Model.Mvvm.Controls.Document`/`Tool` have no `Content` property (confirmed by reflection back in Task 5). What none of those tasks' empirical API investigations turned up — because nothing that far into the branch had a display to check against — is that `Dock.Model.Mvvm`'s dockables don't host whatever's in `Context` directly either. Dock resolves a dockable's on-screen content through an `IDataTemplate` lookup keyed by the dockable's own type, the same mechanism WPF/Avalonia `ContentPresenter`s use for arbitrary view models — `Context` is data to be templated, not a control to be hosted. `DockControl.AutoCreateDataTemplates` (on by default) supplies *some* default template, but it has no idea `Context` here happens to already be a built `NativeWebView`/`ResultsView`, so it renders nothing. Every prior review pass (including the final whole-branch review, which explicitly flagged this exact risk as unverifiable without a display) had no way to catch it — `dotnet build`/`dotnet test` don't exercise Avalonia's template-resolution pipeline at all, and the app launches and reaches "Host window ready" whether or not anything is actually drawn inside the dock panes.

**Fix.** `MainWindow.axaml.fs` registers two `FuncDataTemplate`s on the real `DockControl` before `Layout` is assigned — one for `Dock.Model.Mvvm.Controls.Document`, one for `Tool` — each producing a `ContentControl` whose `Content` is the dockable's own `Context`. Avalonia's `ContentPresenter` recognizes that value is already a `Control` and hosts it directly rather than looking for a further template, so the `NativeWebView`/`ResultsView` instances built in `MainWindow`'s constructor finally reach the screen. Registered ahead of whatever `AutoCreateDataTemplates` supplies so these are the first match Avalonia's template lookup finds for either type.

**Also added while chasing this, at the user's request as a diagnostic:** every freshly created document (the workspace's very first tab and every `File > New`) now seeds with a short F# comment + `printfn` example instead of an empty buffer, so a blank pane and a genuinely-empty-but-rendering editor are no longer visually identical. `DocumentViewModel` gained an optional `initialText` constructor parameter that sets `Text` directly — bypassing `ApplyTextChanged` — so the seed doesn't mark a freshly opened tab dirty for text nobody typed; `Version` stays `0`, matching Monaco's own `currentVersion` on a fresh load, so the guard in `ApplyTextChanged` still accepts the user's first real edit normally.

Three pre-existing `ShellViewModelTests` asserted a second/background/orphaned document's `Text` was `""` — true before this change, no longer true now that every document starts with seed text. Updated to capture the untouched document's text before the exercised operation and assert it's unchanged, rather than asserting a specific literal — the tests' actual intent (routing doesn't touch documents it shouldn't) is unaffected either way.

Build/test: `dotnet build` clean, 0 warnings, 0 errors, full solution. `dotnet test`: same 12 pre-existing Testcontainers/Docker-unavailable failures as every prior round (`Integration.Tests` only, unrelated to this change), everything else green — `Ui.Tests` 135/135.

**Still unverified in this environment, same standing limitation as every prior XAML/WebView task:** that the icons/editor/results actually render correctly on a real screen. The mechanism (an `IDataTemplate` match producing a `ContentControl` around an already-built `Control`) is standard, well-documented Avalonia behavior and not itself risky, but this fix is the first change in the whole branch that could only have been found by someone actually looking at the running app — worth remembering for UI-Workspace: `Avalonia.Headless` rendering tests would have caught this class of bug automatically instead of shipping it to a user's first launch.

Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/MainWindow.axaml.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/DocumentViewModel.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/ShellViewModel.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/ShellViewModelTests.fs`, `docs/DEVLOG.md`.

## 2026-08-10 — Fix: the shared `NativeWebView` design itself was broken — one instance per tab instead

The `DataTemplate` fix above got Monaco on screen, but the user's very next report — still nothing visible, plus `InvalidOperationException: The control NativeWebView already has a visual parent ContentPresenter ... while trying to add it as a child of ContentPresenter ...` the moment a second tab was opened or closed — pointed at something deeper than a missing template.

**Root cause.** Every `ScriptDocument`'s `Context` was, by design since Task 5, the *same* single `webView` instance — the whole rationale being "one Monaco/WebView2 engine, not N of them." That's a fine instinct for the underlying browser engine, but it collided with how Dock actually presents document content: each `ScriptDocument` gets its own `ContentControl` (one per open tab, built by the `FuncDataTemplate` above), and Avalonia's visual tree requires a `Control` to have exactly one parent. The instant two `ScriptDocument`s existed — which `CreateLayout`'s one initial tab never triggered, so nothing caught this in Task 5-9's own work — both tabs' `ContentControl`s tried to claim the identical `NativeWebView`, and the second claim threw. This is exactly the residual risk flagged (but not yet forced to a head) in the final whole-branch review: *"Shared NativeWebView across N ScriptDocument.Context values is safe only while document-tab caching is off ... enabling it ... would throw the moment a second tab opens."* Turns out no caching setting was needed to hit it — Dock realizes more than one tab's content control under ordinary add/remove, not just under an opt-in caching feature.

**Fix.** `DockFactory`'s constructor now takes `createEditorControl: unit -> obj` instead of a fixed `editorContent: obj` — a factory, not an instance. `MainWindow` supplies a closure that builds a brand-new `NativeWebView`, points it at `editor-host.html`, and wires its own `WebMessageReceived` handler, and `DockFactory` calls it once per tab (`CreateLayout`'s initial document, and `addTab` for every `File > New`/`Open` after). `DocumentActivated` now fires the `ScriptDocument` itself rather than just its `DocumentViewModel`, so `MainWindow` can reach that specific tab's own `Context` to push its `DocumentLoaded` notification into the right control — there's no longer a single webview to default to. `ShellViewModel.SendToEditor` (shared by `ProblemViewModel.Navigate` and the post-save re-key) now resolves to whichever tab is active *at send time* via `dockFactory.DocumentDock.ActiveDockable`, since both callers only ever act on the active document and there's no longer one fixed webview to reach directly.

`ResultsView` stays shared — there's exactly one "Results" tool in the whole app, never two competing for the same control, so nothing about it needed to change.

**Known cost, not fixed here:** switching back to an already-open tab still re-pushes `DocumentLoaded` (same as before this change), which calls Monaco's `setValue` and discards that tab's undo stack and cursor position — the pre-existing Minor finding from the final review. Per-tab `NativeWebView`s make "only push once, on first activation" straightforward to add later (each tab now keeps its own live Monaco state across switches once loaded), but that's a UX improvement, not what was crashing — left for a follow-up rather than bundled into an urgent fix.

New regression test: `every open document tab gets its own editor control instance, never shared` (`DockFactoryTests.fs`) — a `DockFactory` built with a counting `createEditorControl`, three tabs opened, asserts three distinct contexts. Three pre-existing `DockFactoryTests`/`LayoutSerializerTests` constructor calls (`DockFactory(shell, obj (), obj ())`) updated to the new factory-function signature; the one `DocumentActivated` test updated to read the `ScriptDocument` payload instead of a bare `DocumentViewModel`.

Build/test: `dotnet build` clean across all 24 projects, 0 warnings, 0 errors. `dotnet test`: `Ui.Tests` 136/136 (up from 135). Full solution: same 12 pre-existing Testcontainers/Docker-unavailable failures as every prior round, unrelated to this change.

**Still unverified in this environment:** whether this actually renders and accepts input on a real screen — same standing limitation as every fix in this branch, and exactly why this bug existed in the first place. Two live bugs in a row now (the missing `DataTemplate`, this one) were only findable by someone running the built app, not by anything `dotnet build`/`dotnet test` exercises — worth treating as a firm argument for `Avalonia.Headless` rendering tests before UI-Workspace starts, not just a nice-to-have.

Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/DockFactory.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/MainWindow.axaml.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/ScriptDocument.fs` (doc comment only), `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/DockFactoryTests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/LayoutSerializerTests.fs`, `docs/DEVLOG.md`.

## 2026-08-10 — Fix: the custom `DataTemplate` fix was targeting the wrong thing entirely — `IDocumentContent`/`IToolContent` were the real contract

Third report in a row on the same underlying symptom: even after the shared-`NativeWebView` crash was fixed, Monaco still never appeared. This time the fix required actually loading `Dock.Avalonia.dll` (the real UI control package, not the `Dock.Model`/`Dock.Model.Mvvm` layers every earlier investigation in this project had reflected on) and calling its internal rendering logic directly, rather than reasoning from documentation snippets a third time.

**Root cause, found by direct experiment, not by reading further docs.** Loaded `Dock.Avalonia.Internal.DockDataTemplateHelper` (the class behind `DockControl.AutoCreateDataTemplates`, on by default) and called `CreateDefaultDataTemplates()` for real. The very first template in the list targets `Dock.Model.Controls.IDocumentContent` — a genuine interface with a real, settable `Content: obj` property — and `Match()` against a plain `Document` with only `Context` set returns `false`. `Dock.Model.Mvvm.Controls.Document` does not implement it. Confirmed with a second probe: a test type implementing `IDocumentContent.Content` matched immediately and built a real `Dock.Avalonia.Controls.DocumentContentControl`. The earlier `FuncDataTemplate<Document>`/`<Tool>` registration (previous entry) was a plausible-looking fix that happened to compile and run without error, but was never the mechanism Dock's own `DocumentContentControl`/`ToolContentControl` machinery actually consults — which is exactly why nothing changed after it shipped.

**Fix.** `ScriptDocument` now implements `Dock.Model.Controls.IDocumentContent`, returning its `editorContent` (unchanged from before) through `Content` instead of `Context` — `Context` is no longer set at all, and `EditorControl` is a new plain member exposing the same object for `MainWindow`'s own bridge-push code, so nothing there needs an interface cast. A new type, `ContentTool`, does the equivalent for the "Results" tool: `Tool` itself can't be extended with a constructor-injected `Content` (its own persistence needs a parameterless constructor — see below), so `ContentTool` is a small subclass with a plain settable `Content` property, `[<IgnoreDataMember>]` for the same reason `DockableBase.Context` already carries that attribute (a live `ResultsView` can't be JSON-serialized — this is the identical failure class Task 12 spent four rounds fixing for `Context`, now avoided up front for `Content`). The hand-rolled `FuncDataTemplate` registrations from the previous fix are removed entirely — `AutoCreateDataTemplates`'s real defaults now do the work, which is what they were always meant to do.

**The one thing this could have broken and didn't:** `ContentTool` is a genuinely new concrete type entering the serialized layout for the first time — unlike `ScriptDocument`, which is structurally excluded from the wire format, `resultsTool` *is* part of the normal persisted layout, restored by `DockFactory.RestoreToolPanes`'s `FindById` lookup. Whether Dock's polymorphic serializer would preserve a custom `Tool` subclass's concrete type through a real round-trip was untested territory. It works: `RestoreToolPanes`'s existing pattern match now requires the restored node to come back as `Some(:? ContentTool as rrt)` rather than accepting any `IDockable`, and the full existing `LayoutSerializerTests` suite — unmodified in its actual assertions, only in the `.Context` → `.Content` property it reads — passed without a single new failure. If the concrete type hadn't survived, the match would have fallen through to `RestoreToolPanes` returning `false`, the same safe "reject this restore, fall back to the default layout" path every other restore failure in this project already degrades into — not a crash.

Three pre-existing tests needed the same mechanical `.Context` → `.EditorControl`/`.Content` rename this fix's own interface change required (`ScriptDocumentTests.fs`, `DockFactoryTests.fs`, `LayoutSerializerTests.fs` — six call sites across the latter, all reading `factory.ResultsTool.Context`), plus one new test added directly: `IDocumentContent.Content is the same editor control instance EditorControl returns`.

Build/test: `dotnet build` clean across all 24 projects, 0 warnings, 0 errors. `dotnet test`: `Ui.Tests` 137/137 (up from 136, including the `ContentTool` round-trip coverage in `LayoutSerializerTests`). Full solution: same 12 pre-existing Testcontainers/Docker-unavailable failures as every prior round, unrelated to this change.

**Still unverified in this environment:** whether Monaco actually renders on a real screen now. Three fixes in a row for the same user-reported symptom, each one genuinely wrong in a different way, each one only discoverable by someone running the built app — the strongest evidence yet in this branch's own history for adding `Avalonia.Headless` rendering tests before UI-Workspace starts, not deferring it as a nice-to-have.

Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/ScriptDocument.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/ContentTool.fs` (new), `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/DockFactory.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/MainWindow.axaml.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.fsproj`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/ScriptDocumentTests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/DockFactoryTests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/LayoutSerializerTests.fs`, `docs/DEVLOG.md`.

## 2026-08-10 — Diagnostics: `AssetHost` never logged its own port or an accept-loop death

User report: `http://127.0.0.1:PORT/` unreachable in a browser while the app was running, suspecting the Monaco asset host wasn't actually running. Investigated directly rather than guessing: built and launched the real app, read the (until now, nonexistent) startup log for the bound port, and `curl`'d it live while the process was still up.

**Findings, empirical, not inferred.** `GET /editor-host.html` → `HTTP 200` with real content; `GET /` → `HTTP 404` (correct — the loopback server only serves known files under `EditorAssets`, by design since A.11). The asset host works exactly as intended. The far more likely explanation for the report: `AssetHost.start` picks a fresh OS-assigned ephemeral port on every single launch, and — until this entry — logged nothing about which port that was. Whatever address the user tried was almost certainly captured from a prior run; once that process exits, nothing is listening on that port anymore, and there was no way to tell "stale port" from "the listener is actually broken" after the fact.

**Fix — pure observability, no behavior change.** `AssetHost.start` now logs `Asset host listening at {BaseUri}` (`Log.Information`) the moment the real listener starts. `acceptLoop`'s catch clause previously handled exactly two exception types (`ObjectDisposedException`/`HttpListenerException`, both normal shutdown signals) and let anything else silently end the loop with zero trace — any other exception now gets `Log.Warning`'d before the loop still stops the same way it always did. Needed adding `Serilog` as a direct dependency of `FSharpQueryStudio.Editor` (it had none before), matching the same direct-`Log.*`-call convention already used in `Worker`/`Ui`/`App` rather than routing through `ILogger<T>`.

**Also surfaced while reading the log, unrelated to this report but worth recording:** the user's last two real launches (before this session's rebuilds) both logged `Saved dock layout passed CanRestore but a graft still failed; using the default layout instead`. Expected, not a new bug — their `ui-state.json` was written by a build from before the `IDocumentContent`/`ContentTool`/shared-`NativeWebView` fixes landed, so the saved object graph no longer matches what the current `DockFactory` builds. `CanRestore`'s whole job (Task 12) is exactly this: reject an incompatible saved layout and fall back to the default rather than corrupt anything. Confirmed safe, not investigated further.

Build/test: `dotnet build` clean, 0 warnings, 0 errors, full solution (25 projects, `Editor` gaining its first `Serilog` reference). `dotnet test`: `Editor.Tests` 24/24 unchanged. Full solution: same 12 pre-existing Testcontainers/Docker-unavailable failures as every prior round.

Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Editor/AssetHost.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Editor/FSharpQueryStudio.Editor.fsproj`, `docs/DEVLOG.md`.

## 2026-08-10 — UNRESOLVED: real content in the dock layout crashes the app with a StackOverflowException

**Status: blocked, not fixed.** After the `IDocumentContent`/`ContentTool` fix (previous entry) made the document/results panes render for the first time, the user reported a new, severe symptom: the app throws a fatal `StackOverflowException` a few seconds after launch. This entry documents everything ruled out so far, so the next attempt doesn't repeat this investigation from scratch.

**The crash is real and reproducible.** `StackOverflowException` is uncatchable in .NET — nothing in the app's own Serilog output can show it, and it never reached Windows Event Log either (checked directly; the only recent fatal-crash event in Event Viewer belonged to an unrelated process). Reproduced directly instead: launched the built app with a bounded timeout and captured raw console output. Every crash shows the identical shape — `Avalonia.Layout.LayoutManager.Arrange(Layoutable)` calling itself thousands of times (7000+), then unwinding through `ExecuteArrangePass` → `ExecuteLayoutPass` → `MediaContext.Render` → the normal dispatcher/message loop. This is Avalonia's own layout system in unbounded self-recursion, not anything in this project's application code throwing.

**Ruled out, each via a real rebuild-and-relaunch test, not reasoning alone:**
- **Not `NativeWebView`.** Swapped `createEditorControl`'s return value for a plain `TextBlock` — identical crash, identical stack shape.
- **Not `ResultsView`/`DataGrid`.** Additionally swapped `resultsView` for a plain `TextBlock` (both controls stubbed at once) — identical crash.
- **Not Dock's own `DocumentContentControl`/`ToolContentControl`.** Registered a custom `FuncDataTemplate<ScriptDocument>`/`<ContentTool>` producing a plain `ContentControl` instead, bypassing Dock's default `IDocumentContent`/`IToolContent`-driven templates entirely (confirmed via the same `DockDataTemplateHelper.CreateDefaultDataTemplates` reflection from the previous entry that these two are what `AutoCreateDataTemplates` actually builds) — identical crash, real `NativeWebView`/`ResultsView` content, same `LayoutManager.Arrange` recursion.
- **Not the asset host / browser interaction the user described.** The crash reproduces with a bounded, non-interactive launch — no browser involved, no request ever reaches `AssetHost`. The "address unreachable" report from the previous entry and this crash are two separate, unrelated symptoms that happened to be reported together.

**What's left, not yet tested:** the surrounding `ProportionalDock`/`DocumentDock`/`ToolDock` nesting `DockFactory.CreateLayout()` builds (horizontal outer split, vertical inner split, `Proportion` values 0.2/0.3, `IsCollapsable = false` on several nodes) is the one variable never isolated — every test so far changed *what* renders inside a pane, never the dock structure itself. A minimal single-pane layout (just `DocumentDock`, no workspace/results panes, no nesting) with real content would either implicate the nesting specifically or rule it out too.

**Also checked and ruled out as an easy fix:** `Avalonia.Controls.WebView` is pinned at 12.0.1 against the rest of Avalonia's 12.1.1/Dock's 12.1.0 — a real version gap, but no 12.1.x release of that package exists yet on NuGet, and the `NativeWebView`-removed test above crashes identically anyway, so this isn't the cause. Found one related, still-open, unfixed upstream report — [wieslawsoltes/Dock#1102](https://github.com/wieslawsoltes/Dock/issues/1102), "InvalidOperationException when using ContentControl inside DocumentDock" — describing Dock 12.x's content-hosting as fragile in ways Dock 11.x wasn't (no comments, no fix). No existing upstream issue matches this specific StackOverflow/Arrange-recursion shape; worth filing one if this remains unresolved, since it may be new information for that project.

**Current code state:** left relying on `AutoCreateDataTemplates`/`IDocumentContent`/`IToolContent` (the architecturally correct approach from the previous entry, not disproven, just insufficient on its own) rather than the custom-`ContentControl` mitigation, since the latter didn't help and added complexity for no benefit. The app still crashes in this state — this entry does not claim a fix, only documents the elimination work done so far.

Build/test: `dotnet build` clean, 0 warnings, 0 errors. `dotnet test`: `Ui.Tests` 137/137 unchanged (this investigation touched only `MainWindow.axaml.fs`'s content-template wiring, reverted to its pre-investigation state; no production behavior changed by this entry beyond the DataTemplate registration attempt, which is not in the final diff).

Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/MainWindow.axaml.fs` (comment only — the diagnostic edits were reverted), `docs/DEVLOG.md`.

## 2026-08-10 — UNRESOLVED (continued): dock nesting ruled out too — the crash is timing-dependent, not structural

**Status: still blocked.** Tested the one variable the previous entry left open: whether `DockFactory.CreateLayout()`'s nested `ProportionalDock`/`ToolDock` structure was the trigger. It is not — the crash happens even in the simplest possible layout, which changes the shape of the problem significantly.

**Method:** temporarily edited `CreateLayout()` (never committed) through four variants, rebuilding and relaunching the real app in the background with a bounded timeout after each, then reading the captured console output:
1. `RootDock` → `DocumentDock` directly, no `ProportionalDock`/`ToolDock`/splitter at all.
2. Single-level `ProportionalDock` (`documentDock` + splitter + `resultsDock`), no outer horizontal wrap, no `workspaceDock`.
3. Full original two-level nesting (horizontal outer, vertical inner, both `ToolDock`s with their real `Alignment`/`Proportion`), reconstructed to match the committed code exactly.

**First pass (15s launches): all three looked clean** — no crash, real `NativeWebView` and `ResultsView` content, asset host up, "Host window ready" logged. This looked like it confirmed the nesting hypothesis. It didn't — the run just wasn't long enough.
