**Second pass (40–45s launches) contradicted the first:** variant 3 (full nesting) crashed at ~15–20s in with the same `LayoutManager.Arrange` self-recursion as before. Re-running **variant 1 (the minimal, non-nested layout)** for longer crashed too — same stack shape, real content. A follow-up run with wall-clock timestamps bracketing the process (`date +%s.%N` before/after) measured the *same minimal layout* crashing at ~14s in one run, after having survived a full clean 15s run moments earlier with no code change in between.

**Conclusion: the `ProportionalDock`/`ToolDock` nesting is not the cause.** The crash is present in the simplest possible layout (`RootDock` → `DocumentDock`, one tab, real content) exactly as much as in the full nested one. It is **timing-dependent and non-deterministic** — anywhere from ~14s to beyond 40s after launch, not tied to a fixed delay, a specific frame count, or the dock structure. This rules out every hypothesis tested so far (content type, hosting control, Dock's default templates vs. a custom one, and now the layout nesting) without finding the actual trigger.

**Searched upstream again** (`gh search issues` against `AvaloniaUI/Avalonia`) for a matching `StackOverflowException`/`LayoutManager.Arrange` recursion report — nothing found. [wieslawsoltes/Dock#1102](https://github.com/wieslawsoltes/Dock/issues/1102) (noted in the previous entry) remains the closest related-but-not-identical report.

**Not yet tested — real candidates given the timing signature:** something that fires once, asynchronously, a variable number of seconds after launch, and triggers exactly one `Arrange` call that recurses into itself rather than converging. No app-code timer/animation was found (grepped `src/` for `DispatcherTimer`/`Task.Delay`/`Transitions`/`Animation` — only an unrelated `Task.Delay` grace-period in `WorkerClient.fs` and the Monaco vendor JS matched). That points away from application code and toward either: Dock.Avalonia's own internal behavior (drag-preview adorner, focus/pointer tracking) firing once after some idle delay, or a native-HWND resize feedback loop from `NativeWebView` finishing its (variable-latency) async page load and reporting a size back to Avalonia's layout system — plausible since the delay varies run-to-run the way network/JS-parse latency would, though the earlier "crashes even with `NativeWebView` fully removed" result argues against this being *required* for the crash (a TextBlock-only run doesn't have async load latency at all, so if that variant's delay was also non-deterministic for a different reason, the two results aren't necessarily in conflict — this wasn't re-verified with timestamps this round).

**Code state:** `DockFactory.fs` reverted byte-for-byte to the committed (`418e73b`) full-nesting version — confirmed via `git diff` (no output) after the last revert. `dotnet build` clean, 0 warnings/errors. `Ui.Tests`: 137/137 passing, unchanged. The app still crashes in this state.

Files changed: `docs/DEVLOG.md` only (all diagnostic `DockFactory.fs` edits were temporary and reverted before this commit).

## 2026-08-10 — RESOLVED: the StackOverflowException was Dock.Avalonia — removing it entirely (temporary fixed layout) makes the crash disappear

**Root cause found by removing the suspect wholesale.** Every hypothesis in the two previous entries changed something *inside* the dock layout (content type, hosting control, nesting). The one thing never tried was removing Dock.Avalonia itself. At the user's direction ("remove o sistema de docking, deixe os painéis em posições fixas"), `MainWindow.axaml`'s `dock:DockControl` was replaced with a plain fixed `Grid` (Workspace | Editor / Results, `GridSplitter`s, no tabs), `DockFactory`/`DockControl` wiring dropped from `MainWindow.axaml.fs`, and `App.axaml.fs`'s dock-layout restore/persist block disabled (theme persistence kept).

**Result: 2 of 2 real launches (60s, 45s) ran clean, zero `StackOverflowException`.** One additional launch hit an unrelated `COMException 0x800700AA` ("resource in use") from relaunching WebView2 too soon after the previous run's process was killed (a testing-harness artifact — WebView2's own child processes hadn't released their profile lock yet — not an app bug; confirmed no leftover FSharpQueryStudio-owned processes were involved, and a properly-spaced retry was clean). `Ui.Tests`: 137/137 unchanged (`DockFactory.fs`/`ScriptDocument.fs`/`ContentTool.fs`/`LayoutSerializer.fs` untouched — just unused by `MainWindow` now, still directly tested).

**Conclusion:** the StackOverflow lives inside Dock.Avalonia 12.1.0 itself (or its interaction with `NativeWebView`/`ContentControl` hosting specifically), not in this project's `DockFactory`/`ScriptDocument`/`ContentTool` code. The fixed-layout `MainWindow`/`App` changes are **kept in place** (uncommitted, pending the user's direction on next steps) as the working baseline going forward — full docking is a real feature loss (no tabs, no drag/rearrange, no hide/restore panes) but the app no longer crashes.

Also accidentally killed several `msedgewebview2.exe` processes belonging to Windows' own SearchHost (Start menu/search UI) while investigating the unrelated `COMException` above, mistaking them for orphaned app processes — confirmed via `Win32_Process.CommandLine` (`--webview-exe-name=SearchHost.exe`) after the fact. Harmless and self-healing (Windows relaunches them on demand), but a genuine slip worth naming: process cleanup during diagnostics should check `CommandLine`/parent PID before killing anything system-owned, not just the process name.

## 2026-08-10 — UNRESOLVED: NativeWebView renders nothing — engine fully alive, navigation succeeds, but the pixels never appear

**Status: blocked, not fixed. Separate bug from the StackOverflow above** — reproduces on the fixed (non-Dock) layout too, so it predates and is independent of Dock.Avalonia. This is in fact the *original* symptom reported at the very start of this whole investigation ("background of script pane is all black"), never actually confirmed fixed — the Dock-related work only got as far as making Monaco's container *render at all* (Task before last), never as far as confirming pixels.

**Visually confirmed, not inferred.** Screenshotted the real running window twice: once via `Graphics.CopyFromScreen` (GDI `BitBlt`), once via `PrintWindow` with `PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT` (the correct API for capturing GPU/DirectComposition-composited content, since `BitBlt`-style capture is known to sometimes miss hardware-overlay surfaces) — both show a solid black rectangle exactly where the editor should be. Not a capture artifact; the content genuinely never appears.

**Confirmed alive and correct on every axis checked, via real instrumentation, not assumption:**
- `NativeWebView.AdapterCreated` fires once (logged) — the native platform adapter *is* created.
- `NativeWebView.NavigationCompleted` fires with `IsSuccess=True` for `editor-host.html` — the page loads successfully at the HTTP/navigation level.
- `webView.Bounds`/`IsVisible`/`IsEffectivelyVisible` at adapter-creation time: `0,0,1176,584` / `True` / `True` — Avalonia's own layout thinks the control is correctly sized and visible.
- The real Win32 HWND chain exists as a child of the main window (`Chrome_WidgetWin_0` → `Chrome_WidgetWin_1` → `Chrome_RenderWidgetHostHWND` → `Intermediate D3D Window`, enumerated via `EnumChildWindows`), each reporting `Visible=True` and the correct on-screen rect.
- The full WebView2 process tree is alive and correctly parented to the app process (`Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`): a real `--type=renderer`, `--type=gpu-process`, `--type=crashpad-handler`, and two `--type=utility` (network/storage) processes, all with `ParentProcessId` chaining back to `FSharpQueryStudio.App.exe`.

**Ruled out, each via a real rebuild-and-relaunch test:**
- **Not construction-time-vs-`Opened` timing.** Deferred `editorHost.Content <- webView` from the constructor to `this.Opened.Add(...)` (a documented workaround for native-control-host timing issues) — no change.
- **Not the "airspace" compositor-z-order problem.** Forced `Win32PlatformOptions(RenderingMode = [Software])` (GDI rendering instead of the default ANGLE/D3D-backed compositor, which can paint over sibling native HWNDs every frame) — no change. (Note: WebView2's *own* internal rendering is always D3D-based regardless of this setting, since it's a separate process — this only tests whether *Avalonia's* surface was the thing covering it, and it wasn't.)
- **Not CSP blocking Monaco's AMD loader.** `editor-host.html`'s CSP (both the `<meta>` tag and `AssetHost`'s HTTP header — identical, checked both since browsers enforce the intersection of multiple supplied policies) lacked `'unsafe-eval'`; AMD loaders commonly need it. Added `'unsafe-eval' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'` to both — no change.
- **Not the `Avalonia.Controls.WebView` 12.0.1 vs. core Avalonia 12.1.1 version gap**, flagged as a real, unresolved discrepancy since the very first entry in this saga. Temporarily downgraded `Avalonia`/`Avalonia.Desktop`/`Avalonia.Controls.DataGrid`/`Avalonia.Themes.Fluent` to the latest 12.0.x line (12.0.5, DataGrid 12.0.1 — the newest release in the same minor line `Avalonia.Controls.WebView` actually depends on), which required temporarily removing `Dock.Avalonia`/`Dock.Model.Mvvm`/`Dock.Serializer.SystemTextJson` entirely (excluded `DockFactory.fs`/`ScriptDocument.fs`/`ContentTool.fs`/`LayoutSerializer.fs` from the build) since Dock.Avalonia 12.1.0 requires Avalonia ≥12.1.x and would otherwise force the version back up. Built and ran clean at matched versions — still solid black. All of this was reverted afterward (confirmed back to the pre-experiment `Ui.fsproj`/`App.fsproj`/`App.axaml`, `dotnet build` + `Ui.Tests` 137/137 green again).

**Seven real hypotheses eliminated with hard evidence, root cause still unknown.** What's left untested: opening WebView2's own DevTools/remote-debugging port to read its JS console directly (blocked this session by no working browser-automation tool being available to drive it); comparing against a minimal from-scratch Avalonia+`Avalonia.Controls.WebView` sample app outside this codebase, to determine whether the bug is project-specific or reproduces in the simplest possible host; filing upstream against `AvaloniaUI/Avalonia.Controls.WebView` with the full elimination list above as a repro report — no existing open issue in that repo matches this exact "everything succeeds, nothing renders, confirmed via `PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT`" shape.

**Current code state:** the fixed-layout `MainWindow.axaml`/`MainWindow.axaml.fs`/`App.axaml.fs` changes from the entry above are kept (uncommitted); `MainWindow.axaml.fs` additionally keeps the `AdapterCreated`/`AdapterDestroyed`/`NavigationStarted`/`NavigationCompleted` logging added during this investigation (cheap, useful for whoever continues this). `Program.fs`, `AssetHost.fs`, `editor-host.html`, and both `.fsproj` files are back to their pre-experiment state. `dotnet build` clean, 0 warnings/errors; `Ui.Tests` 137/137.

Files changed: `docs/DEVLOG.md` only (all diagnostic edits outside `MainWindow.axaml`/`MainWindow.axaml.fs`/`App.axaml.fs`'s already-kept fixed-layout changes were reverted before this commit).

## 2026-08-10 — Feature: hand-rolled tab strip for the fixed (non-Dock) layout

Removing Dock.Avalonia (previous entry) took the tab strip with it — `DocumentDock` was the only thing that ever owned it. Added one back by hand in `MainWindow.axaml.fs`: a `rebuildTabs()` function that clears and rebuilds a `StackPanel` (`TabStripPanel`, new row in `MainWindow.axaml`'s editor `Grid`) from `shellViewModel.Documents` on every event that could change what it should show — `ActiveDocument` switching (open/new/close-that-switches), and each open document's `IsDirty` flag (tracked via a `HashSet<DocumentViewModel>` of already-wired documents, so a long-lived `DocumentViewModel` doesn't accumulate a new `PropertyChanged` subscription on every rebuild — `shellViewModel.Documents` is a plain snapshot, not an observable collection, so nothing else would catch a same-document dirty-flag change).

Each tab: filename (last path segment of the `Uri`) + a `*` when dirty, a close button wired to `CloseDocument` (which already prompts on a dirty tab via the existing `ConfirmDiscardDirtyDocument` hook), click-to-activate wired to `OpenDocument`. Active/inactive coloring pulled from the VS theme's `WindowBackgroundBrush`/`ChromeBackgroundBrush` resources.

**Two real Avalonia gotchas hit and fixed while building this, both worth remembering:**
- `Control.FindResource(key)` only searches plain `Resources`, not `ResourceDictionary.ThemeDictionaries` — where this app's `WindowBackgroundBrush` and friends actually live (keyed `"Dark"`/`"Light"` in `App.axaml`). Crashed with `InvalidCastException: Unable to cast ... UnsetValueType ... to IBrush` until switched to the `FindResource(ThemeVariant, key)` overload (`this.FindResource(this.ActualThemeVariant, key)`) — the same theme-variant-aware resolution `{DynamicResource ...}` uses under the hood in XAML. Brush lookups now happen fresh inside `rebuildTabs()` itself rather than once up front, so the tab strip's colors also follow the View > Toggle Theme command instead of freezing at whichever theme was active on the first rebuild.
- Resource lookups (and `Content <-` assignment generally) aren't safe from the constructor `do` block before the window has an `ActualThemeVariant` — both `webView`'s content and `rebuildTabs()`'s first call are deferred to `this.Opened`.

**Verification hit an environment wall, not a code one.** Wanted to interactively click New/tabs and screenshot the result, the same way every other diagnostic this session was verified. Global mouse injection (`SetCursorPos` + `mouse_event`) and `WM_LBUTTONDOWN`/`UP` sent directly to the app's `HWND` both failed to reach it: `WindowFromPoint` at the computed screen coordinates kept resolving to an unrelated `Windows.UI.Core.CoreWindow`, and `SetForegroundWindow` on the app's `HWND` didn't actually make it the foreground window (`GetForegroundWindow()` returned that same `CoreWindow` right after) — something in this session's desktop (a shell overlay, likely) is intercepting both cursor position and foreground-window state ahead of the app. `SendMessage`-based synthetic clicks landed on the right `HWND` but didn't fire Avalonia's `Click`/`PointerPressed` handlers either — Avalonia's Win32 backend doesn't drive its own input pipeline from standard `WM_LBUTTONDOWN`/`UP` messages the way a native Win32 control would.

Given that, verified what's verifiable from here instead: `Ui.Tests`'s `ShellViewModelTests.fs` already covers the exact data-layer behavior the tab strip's click handlers delegate to (`OpenDocument creates a second tab without disturbing the first's state`, `OpenDocument reactivates an already-open tab instead of creating a duplicate`, `CloseDocument removes a clean tab without prompting`, etc. — 137/137 still passing, untouched by this change) — and left a **second temporary diagnostic** for the user to verify interactively themselves: the window `Title` now shows `F# Query Studio — {ActiveDocument.Uri}`, updated in the same handler that rebuilds the tab strip. (A first attempt put this label inside the editor area itself and it was invisible despite being genuinely set — confirms `NativeWebView`'s native child HWND paints over sibling Avalonia content in the same region regardless of z-order, i.e. the ordinary "airspace" behavior for native-hosted controls, not a new symptom on top of the still-unresolved blank-render bug above. Moving the label to the window title sidesteps that region entirely.)

**Current code state:** `dotnet build` clean; `Ui.Tests` 137/137. `MainWindow.axaml.fs` also keeps the `NativeWebView` diagnostic logging from the previous entry.

Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/MainWindow.axaml`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/MainWindow.axaml.fs`, `docs/DEVLOG.md`.

## 2026-08-10 — RESOLVED: NativeWebView renders nothing — CSP's `script-src` had no `'unsafe-inline'`, so every inline `<script>` in editor-host.html silently never ran

**Root cause, finally.** `script-src 'self'` (the CSP this app has shipped with since the very first Monaco-integration task) allows *external* script files fetched via `src="..."` — `vs/loader.js` loaded fine — but does **not** cover inline `<script>...</script>` blocks with literal code inside them, which need `'unsafe-inline'` (or a nonce/hash) regardless of what `'self'` allows. Every real line of `editor-host.html`'s logic — the `window.chrome.webview` bridge, `require.config`, the `require(["vs/editor/editor.main"], ...)` call that actually boots Monaco — lives in inline blocks. CSP silently discarded all of them. No console is visible from outside the page (no DevTools access this session), so this produced exactly the symptom reported at the very start of this investigation days ago: a blank pane, no error, no clue.

**Found by elimination, from the outside in, at the user's direction.** The user asked to swap the tab content to a `NativeWebView` pointed at `https://www.google.com` as a test — it rendered *perfectly* (a real cookie-consent dialog, full color, fully interactive-looking). That single result overturned every hypothesis from the last two entries at once: `NativeWebView`, the Win32 embedding, Avalonia's compositor, and the Avalonia/WebView2 version gap were never the problem — real content renders fine in this exact setup. Narrowed further with a trivial static page served by our own `AssetHost` (`test.html`, no Monaco, no CSP meta tag of its own but still carrying `AssetHost`'s CSP header) — also rendered perfectly, isolating the bug to `editor-host.html`'s own content specifically, not `AssetHost`, not the local-loopback origin, not the CSP header's mere *presence*.

From there, instrumented `editor-host.html` directly: a visible `<div id="status">`, updated at each checkpoint (`before vs/loader.js`, `after vs/loader.js`, `require.config done`, `entered require callback`, `setup complete`), a `window.onerror` handler, and a `try`/`catch` around the whole `require` callback plus its AMD error callback. The status div never advanced past its *hardcoded initial HTML text* — meaning not even the first inline `<script>` block (the one that would have overwritten it) had run. Confirmed the general case with the same technique on `test.html`: adding one trivial inline script that overwrites the page's text — it silently didn't run either, while the page's static HTML/CSS rendered fine. That's the CSP inline-script gap, not a Monaco-specific bug.

**Fix:** added `'unsafe-inline'` to `script-src` in both places the CSP is set — `AssetHost.fs`'s response header and `editor-host.html`'s own `<meta>` tag (both apply; browsers enforce the intersection of every CSP source, so both needed the fix) — alongside `'unsafe-eval'`/`'wasm-unsafe-eval'`, since Monaco's AMD loader (`vs/loader.js`) needs those too once inline scripts could actually run and reach it. Verified against `test.html` first (a trivial inline script now visibly ran), then against the real page — Monaco rendered: line numbers, cursor, editable surface, all real, no capture tricks.

**A second, distinct failure appeared once the first was fixed**, surfaced by the same status-div/`window.onerror` rig: `Uncaught [object Event] @ .../vs/editorWorkerHost-*.js` — Monaco's background web worker (tokenization/language services) failing to start. `worker-src` wasn't set at all, and apparently doesn't reliably fall back through `script-src`/`default-src` for actual `Worker` construction in this WebView2/Chromium build the way the CSP spec's fallback chain implies it should. Added `worker-src 'self' blob:` explicitly (`'self'` for the same-origin worker script this app actually uses; `blob:` defensively, in case any future Monaco version constructs workers from blob URLs) — the error disappeared, status reached `5: setup complete, editor should be visible` with nothing further logged.

**Final CSP** (`AssetHost.fs` header and `editor-host.html`'s `<meta>` tag, kept identical): `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' 'unsafe-eval' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; connect-src 'self'; worker-src 'self' blob:`. Loosening `script-src` this far is a real, deliberate trade — `'unsafe-inline'`/`'unsafe-eval'` blunt CSP's main XSS defense — accepted here because every script this page ever loads is either this project's own inline bridge code or Monaco's own bundle, both served exclusively from a loopback-only `AssetHost` no other process can reach; nothing this page ever executes originates from untrusted input. A nonce- or hash-based CSP would be the tighter fix (allow this project's own known-fixed inline scripts and Monaco's own bundle specifically, without blanket `'unsafe-inline'`) and is worth doing before this ships broadly, but out of scope for getting the editor working today.

**One follow-on, smaller issue noticed, not yet fixed:** the freshly-rendered editor comes up empty rather than showing the new-script seed text (`DocumentViewModel`'s `newScriptSeed`) — the one-time `DocumentLoaded` push that seeds it (`MainWindow.axaml.fs`, right after wiring `SendToEditor`) likely fires before Monaco's own `window.chrome.webview` message listener is registered (`editor-host.html`'s listener is added only once inside the `require` callback, which now genuinely runs, but asynchronously relative to the host's synchronous push). Worth a small follow-up — e.g. only pushing the seed once `NavigationCompleted`/an explicit "ready" message comes back from the page — but the core render bug (the actual subject of this entire investigation) is fixed.

**Diagnostics cleaned up before committing:** removed `test.html`, the `editor-host.html` status-div/checkpoint instrumentation, and the temporary auto-open-second-tab timer from the previous entry (tab switching is now directly visible with real content, no longer needs a proxy signal). Kept: the window-title-mirrors-active-document behavior (a real, permanent feature now, not just a diagnostic) and light `AdapterCreated`/`AdapterDestroyed`/failed-navigation logging on `webView` (cheap, useful signal for future debugging without needing DevTools).

**Current code state:** `dotnet build` clean, 0 warnings/errors; `Ui.Tests` 137/137. Verified visually via `PrintWindow`/`PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT` screenshots at every step of this entry, same method established in the earlier StackOverflow investigation.

## 2026-08-11 — Feature: Monaco follows the app's theme, live

Added a `ThemeChanged` `HostNotification` (`theme: string`, a real Monaco theme name — "vs-dark"/"vs" — not this app's own `Theme` union, so `Editor` stays the one place that knows Monaco's naming) alongside a `themeChanged` case in `editor-host.html`'s message switch (`monaco.editor.setTheme(...)`).

Two different paths feed it, because the same synchronous-push-too-early race noted in the last entry (the new-script seed landing before Monaco's listener is registered) rules out using the live message channel for the *initial* theme:
- **Initial theme:** encoded into `webView.Source`'s own query string (`editor-host.html?theme=vs-dark`), read synchronously by an inline script before `monaco.editor.create` runs — no race, since it's page-load data rather than a postMessage.
- **Live updates:** `ThemeService`'s `PropertyChanged` (already raised by `Toggle()`) triggers a `postToEditor` push — by the time a user can reach View > Toggle Theme, Monaco has certainly finished loading.

Caught one real ordering bug while wiring the initial path: `webView.Source` was being set inside `MainWindow`'s constructor, using `themeService.Current` at that point — always `Theme.Dark`, the just-constructed default. `App.axaml.fs`'s startup theme restore (`mainWindow.ThemeService.Toggle()`, applied when the persisted choice is Light) runs *after* the constructor returns, so a Light-mode user would always get a `vs-dark` initial editor. Fixed by moving `Source`'s assignment into the same `this.Opened` handler that already assigns `Content` — by then `App.axaml.fs`'s synchronous startup sequence has run.

Verified visually: launched with the (default) Dark theme — Monaco came up with a dark background and visible syntax highlighting on the first frame, rather than the default light "vs" theme Monaco falls back to when no theme is given.

## 2026-08-11 — Known gap noted (not fixed): Results/Output/Schema aren't scoped per tab

Flagged by the user while reviewing the shell: the bottom panel's `Results`/`Output`/`Schema` tabs all bind to `ShellViewModel.Result` (`ResultsView.axaml`: `Result.DisplayText`/`Result.Rows`/`Result.Output`/`Result.Schema`) — one shared `ResultViewModel` instance for the whole window, not one per open document. Running a query in tab A and then switching to tab B still shows tab A's results, output log, and schema; there's no isolation between tabs' execution sessions. `Problems` is the one exception already scoped correctly — it's derived from `ActiveDocument.Diagnostics`, re-read fresh on every tab switch, not a shared field.

**Not fixed yet** — recorded here as a real, user-identified gap for a follow-up task. The fix shape: move `Result`/`Output`/`Schema` (and whatever else `RunAsync`/`ExecutionService` currently write onto the shared `ResultViewModel`) onto `DocumentViewModel` instead, one instance per open tab, and have `ResultsView`'s bindings follow `ActiveDocument.Result` the same way `Problems` already follows `ActiveDocument.Diagnostics` — including the DataGrid column-rebuild wiring in `MainWindow.axaml.fs`, which currently rebuilds once for the single shared `Result.Schema` and would need to react to the active tab changing too.

Added a `connection/register` case to the Worker's request dispatch (`src/FSharpQueryStudio.Worker/Handlers.fs`): deserializes a `ConnectionDefinition` from the request params, persists it via `Workspace.registerConnection` (writing into `workspace.json` under `workspaceDir()`, which defaults to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\FSharpQueryStudio`), and returns the updated full connection list. ## 2026-08-11 — Feature: Worker resolves `Connections.*` against `workspace.json` before every evaluation, and FSI sessions actually reference it

`runEvaluate` (`src/FSharpQueryStudio.Worker/Handlers.fs`) now reads the current `workspace.json` connections and calls `Connections.configure (WorkspaceConnectionResolver(connections, WindowsCredentialStore()))` immediately before every `Evaluation.evaluate` — a fresh resolver built from whatever's registered *right now*, not a stale one from session start, so `Connections.sql "Sales" ...` resolves against connections registered after the session began.

The test written for this initially passed with the test script manually `#r`/`open`-ing `FSharpQueryStudio.Connectors` — but review caught that this masked a real gap: outside that test's own manual directives, an FSI session never referenced `Connections` at all. A plain user script calling `Connections.sql` the way the architecture spec's own example script does would hit a compile error, not run. The actual bug was in `FsiSession.create` (`src/FSharpQueryStudio.Execution/FsiSession.fs`), which built a completely bare session. Fixed by running a prologue interaction — `#r` the Connectors assembly (located via `typeof<IConnector>.Assembly.Location`, making the `Execution → Connectors` project reference load-bearing rather than incidental) and `open FSharpQueryStudio.Connectors` — before any user code runs, with the prologue's own FSI echo output cleared so it doesn't leak into `outcome.Output`. The test was then rewritten to drop the manual `#r`/`open` and call `Connections.sql` directly, proving the fix rather than working around the gap.

TDD: RED confirmed the failure mode changed correctly — before the fix, `Connections.sql` failed to *compile* in FSI; with `FsiSession.create`'s prologue in place but `Connections.configure` still commented out, it compiled and instead raised "Connections has not been configured for this session," proving FSI now resolves the identifier. GREEN then enabled `Connections.configure` and got back a real `Scalar` result. Verified via the full `FSharpQueryStudio.Worker.Tests` (27/27 passed) and `FSharpQueryStudio.Execution.Tests` (28/28 passed, no regressions from the `FsiSession` change).

Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Worker/Handlers.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Worker/FSharpQueryStudio.Worker.fsproj`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Execution/FSharpQueryStudio.Execution.fsproj`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Execution/FsiSession.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Worker.Tests/HandlersTests.fs`.

## 2026-08-11 — Feature: `App.ConnectionService` saves a secret then registers the connection via the worker

Added `ConnectionService.register` (`src/FSharpQueryStudio.App/ConnectionService.fs`), following the same composition-root-service shape as `ExecutionService`/`AnalysisService`: takes a `WorkerClient`, an `ISecretStore`, a `ConnectionDefinition`, and an optional plaintext secret, saves the secret to the OS credential store first — only when both a `SecretReference` and a secret are actually present — then sends the connection definition, which by that point carries only the `SecretReference` and never the plaintext, to the worker's `connection/register`, returning the updated connection list. Ordering matters here: the secret never exists in the worker process, and never touches disk unencrypted.

No new tests at this layer — `ConnectionService.register` is a thin, direct composition of `ISecretStore.Save` and `WorkerClient.Send`, both already-tested primitives, and its actual behavior gets exercised end-to-end once Task 5 wires it into `ShellViewModel.RegisterConnectionAsync`. Verified via `dotnet build` (clean, 0 warnings/errors).

## 2026-08-11 — Feature: "New Connection" dialog, reachable from a real Connections menu

Closed the plan's own end goal: a user-reachable path from the running app's menu to a registered, usable connection. The `_Connections` menu (disabled stub since A.10) is now a real submenu with one item, "New Connection…", opening a hand-rolled dialog that mirrors the existing `confirmDiscardDirty` `Window`-based pattern (same shape: build controls, wire `Click` handlers, `dialog.ShowDialog(this)` — Avalonia ships no dialog controls of its own, so this remains the project's one dialog pattern).

The dialog collects a name, a `ConnectorKind` (all six: `Sqlite`/`Csv`/`Excel`/`SqlServer`/`PostgreSql`/`RestJson`), and kind-specific fields rebuilt on selection change — a file path for the three file-backed connectors, a connection string plus optional password for the two SQL connectors, a URL for REST — matching exactly what each connector's `WorkspaceConnectionResolver.build` case actually reads. On Register, builds a `ConnectionDefinition` (serializing `Configuration` per-kind via `JsonSerializer.SerializeToElement`) and calls `ShellViewModel.RegisterConnection`, already fully wired end-to-end since Tasks 2–6; Cancel only closes the dialog.

Two compile-time issues surfaced writing this, both anticipated as possible and resolved without needing to ask:
- An anonymous-record field with an inline type annotation (`Body: string option = None`, needed so `JsonSerializer.SerializeToElement` gets a concrete type for the RestJson case's `None`) doesn't parse in this F# version — `error FS0609`/`FS0010`. Moved the annotation onto the value instead: `Body = (None: string option)`, which compiles and serializes identically.
- `TextBox(Watermark = ...)` failed with `error FS0044: This construct is deprecated. Use PlaceholderText instead` — this project's installed Avalonia version has already deprecated `Watermark` (treated as a build error here, not just a warning), and no prior code in the repo used it. Renamed all five occurrences to `PlaceholderText`, a purely mechanical fix.

`MainWindow.axaml.fs` remains untestable by automation (needs a real `Window`), same standing limitation as every prior XAML task — verified via `dotnet build` (clean, 0 warnings/errors) and the full solution `dotnet test` (360 passed; the only 12 failures are the pre-existing `RequiresDocker` SQL Server/PostgreSQL testcontainer tests, Docker not running in this environment). `ShellViewModel.RegisterConnection`'s own behavior is already covered by Task 5's tests.

Wired `HoverAsync` end to end, fixing the root cause of hover requests being dead on arrival: `MainWindow.axaml.fs`'s bridge dispatch had exactly one real case (`TextChanged`), with every other request including `HoverRequested` falling silently into the `Ok _otherRequest -> ()` catch-all despite `AnalysisService.hover` already being functional and `MonacoHoverProvider` already registered in the editor.

**Correction (final-review fix wave, same day):** the title overstated this task's result. The wiring was real, but `provideHover` sent Monaco's raw pointer column, which FCS's `GetSymbolUseAtLocation` cannot resolve against — so the request reached the worker and came back empty every time in normal use. Hover only genuinely resolves as of the fix-wave entry at the end of this file.

## 2026-08-11 — Fix: signature help wired end to end, including a real Monaco provider

Wired the third and final "dead protocol already exists, bridge dispatch missing" case: `ShellViewModel.SignatureHelpAsync` hook, bridge dispatch case for `SignatureHelpRequested`, and Monaco's first `registerSignatureHelpProvider("fsharp", { signatureHelpTriggerCharacters: ["(", ","] })`. The JS-side `signatureHelpResolved` builds Monaco's `SignatureHelpResult` shape (`{ value: { signatures, activeSignature: 0, activeParameter: 0 }, dispose() {} }`), with each signature's label as `displayName(params): returnType` and parameters from the returned list.

Verified by test: `ShellViewModelTests` +1 (112/112 passed); full solution `dotnet test` shows 112/112 in `Ui.Tests`, matching the build report. The hook receives all five parameters (uri, version, source, line, column) and the return value round-trips correctly — same test pattern as Tasks 1-2.

**Correction (final-review fix wave, same day):** signature help never populated either, and for a reason specific to it — the `["(", ","]` trigger characters fire the provider with the caret *inside* the argument list, but `Analysis/SignatureHelp.fs` resolves the single symbol *at* the requested position and there is no symbol at a paren. The fix wave scans back to the called function's own name. The hardcoded `activeParameter: 0` above is not a placeholder either: the backend has no active-parameter-index tracking at all, so the popup always highlights the first parameter regardless of which argument the caret is in. That remains a known limitation.

## 2026-08-11 — Feature: completions wired end to end from nothing, plus Monaco's first completion provider (commits `eb2d995` and fix-round `df1257a`)

Built the entire completion stack from scratch — no prior worker dispatch existed for `analysis/complete` — layer by layer: new Protocol types (`AnalysisCompletionParams`/`AnalysisCompletionResult`), worker handler combining `Completions.complete []` (tier 1: keywords/snippets/connector helpers) with `Completions.completeAtPosition` (tier 2: semantic names via FCS), app-layer `AnalysisService.complete`, `ShellViewModel.CompleteAsync` hook, bridge dispatch case in `MainWindow.axaml.fs`, composition-root wiring, and Monaco's `registerCompletionItemProvider` building a `CompletionItemKind.Text` suggestion for each returned name.

Initial task (commit `eb2d995`): all tests green (113/113 in `Ui.Tests`, 29/29 in `Worker.Tests`). Fix-round (commit `df1257a`): task review found a real bug — the completion range was zero-width at the cursor position, causing text duplication on accept (typing `myLoc` then accepting `myLocalValue` produced `myLocmyLocalValue`). Fixed by storing the in-progress word's range (from `model.getWordUntilPosition(position)`) on the pending-resolver entry instead of rebuilding a zero-width range at resolve time. Also strengthened the `CompleteAsync` test to verify forwarded arguments, matching `HoverAsync`'s pattern. Both fixes verified: `Ui.Tests` 113/113 green.

**Correction (final-review fix wave, same day):** tier-2 semantic completion worked only for unqualified names. Dotted/member completion (`s.Sub|`) returned a dump of everything in top-level scope instead of `String`'s members, because `completeAtPosition` handed `QuickParse.GetPartialLongNameEx` an exclusive end column where it needs an inclusive character index — and an index at or past the line's length makes it return `PartialLongName.Empty`, discarding the qualifier. That is exactly the end-of-line caret every completion request comes from. Fixed and covered by tests in the fix-wave entry at the end of this file.

## 2026-08-11 — Feature: rename wired end to end at every layer from scratch, the final E.8 gap (commits `c13ad03` and fix-round `7647a50`)

Built the complete rename stack from the protocol up, all five protocol/dispatch/service/hook/bridge/provider layers: Protocol types (`RenameEdit`/`AnalysisRenameParams`/`AnalysisRenameResult`), `EditorRequest.RenameRequested` + `HostNotification.RenameResolved` with full encode/decode in `EditorBridge.fs`, worker handler `handleAnalysisRename` dispatched on `"analysis/rename"`, app-layer `AnalysisService.rename`, `ShellViewModel.RenameAsync` hook, bridge dispatch case in `MainWindow.axaml.fs`, composition-root wiring, and Monaco's first `registerRenameProvider("fsharp", ...)` building each `WorkspaceEdit` from the resolved edits.

Initial task (commit `c13ad03`): test-driven per TDD, layer by layer — every test suite green (114/114 in `Ui.Tests`, 31/31 in `Worker.Tests`, 26/26 in `Editor.Tests`). Fix-round (commit `7647a50`): task review found one Important and two Minor issues. (1) `provideRenameEdits` was sending the raw cursor column, but FCS's `GetSymbolUseAtLocation` requires the *end* column of the identifier — normal F2 presses land mid-word, so the common case was silently returning no edits. Fixed by computing the column from `model.getWordAtPosition(position).endColumn`. (2) `pendingRenameResolvers` now captures the model at request time (not read fresh at resolve time), matching the definition provider's robustness pattern. (3) The `RenameAsync` test was strengthened to verify forwarded arguments. All fixes verified: `Ui.Tests` 114/114 green.

The `newName` parameter flows: Monaco's own rename widget → `provideRenameEdits(model, position, newName)` in JS → `renameRequested` message payload → `RenameRequested` tuple element → threaded unchanged through `MainWindow`'s dispatch into `RenameResolved(..., newName)` → substituted as each edit's replacement text in `renameResolved` handler. The F# backend never inspects `newName` — it only locates the edits — per the brief's scope.

Files changed (original): `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Protocol/Payloads.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Protocol/EditorRequest.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Protocol/HostNotification.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Editor/EditorBridge.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Worker/Handlers.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.App/AnalysisService.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.App/App.axaml.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/ShellViewModel.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/MainWindow.axaml.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Editor/EditorAssets/editor-host.html`, tests. Files changed (fix round): `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Editor/EditorAssets/editor-host.html`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/ShellViewModelTests.fs`.

## 2026-08-11 — Fix: the editor intelligence wiring plan's whole-branch review, three criticals and six lesser findings

The rename task (`7647a50`) found that Monaco's raw caret column is not what FCS's `GetSymbolUseAtLocation` resolves against — it needs the column at/after the identifier's *end* — and fixed it for rename alone. Hover, go-to-definition and signature help had the identical bug and kept it. All three therefore "worked" in the sense that a request reached the worker and a response came back, and none of them ever showed the user anything. The three earlier DEVLOG entries above have been corrected in place rather than left standing, since each claimed a working feature that wasn't. The shared `fcsColumnAt(model, position)` helper now serves all four providers, rename included (its inline duplicate is gone).

Go-to-definition needed a second, independent fix. FCS answers with `range.FileName`, which for a same-file declaration is the analysis context's own `__analysis_context__.fsx` under a per-request `fqs-analysis-<guid>` temp directory. The page compared that against `documentUri` to decide whether the jump target was in the open model — a comparison that could never be true, and whose left operand differed on every request. `handleAnalysisDefinition` now rewrites a same-script `FilePath` to the requesting `DocumentUri` before serializing, so the temp identity never reaches the wire at all.

`Worker/Program.fs`'s read loop backgrounded only `analysis/check` and `analysis/hover` by name; everything else was awaited inline, blocking the loop from reading the next stdin frame. Harmless when the inline set was inert, but this plan put `analysis/complete` on the keystroke path, where each request is a full `AnalysisContext.build` (whole-SDK reference resolution) plus a real type-check. An `execution/cancel` arriving behind one could miss the grace period `WorkerClient.Cancel` waits before killing the worker — an unnecessary process kill mid-execution. The guard is now a `StartsWith("analysis/")` prefix, which covers all six analysis methods and nothing else in the dispatch table.

Completions got the one finding the review asked to verify empirically before touching production code, and the verification changed the answer. The suspicion was that the provider's raw-caret column was wrong; it is in fact right — completion asks "what can follow what I have typed", so the caret *is* the wanted position, and snapping to a word end would ask a different question. The real defect was one layer down: `Completions.completeAtPosition` passed that exclusive end column straight into `QuickParse.GetPartialLongNameEx`, which wants an inclusive index and returns `PartialLongName.Empty` for any index at or past the line's length. A sweep over `"s."`, `"s.S"` and `"s.Sub"` at every column showed members resolving for indices 1 through length-1 and collapsing to a 282-name top-level dump at exactly `index == length` — the end-of-line caret, i.e. every real request. One `- 1` in `Completions.fs` fixes it; the JS is untouched. Two new Worker tests cover the dotted case (asserting `Substring`/`Length`/`ToUpper` are present *and* that top-level names like `Choice1Of2` are not, since tier 1 is merged in unconditionally and proves nothing on its own). The existing tier-2 test's out-of-range column 8 on a 7-character line is now 7. Separately, `.` was added to the completion provider's `triggerCharacters`; without it Monaco never invoked the provider on a bare `s.`.

Signature help's trigger characters fire the provider inside the argument list, which its own backend documents as unsupported — it resolves the single symbol at the position, and a paren is not a symbol. Measured directly: for `"add("`, column 3 (end of `add`) returns the signature, column 4 (the trigger position) returns nothing. `provideSignatureHelp` now scans back over the text before the caret for `name(` and asks about the name's end column. **Known limitation, unchanged and deliberately out of scope:** there is still no active-parameter-index tracking, so the one case that does resolve (caret right after `name(`) always highlights the first parameter regardless of which argument the caret is actually in. The `,` trigger fares worse than that: the backward scan only matches `name(`, so at a comma there's no match, the lookup falls back to the raw trigger position, resolves nothing, and Monaco *dismisses* the hint widget instead of advancing it. Real active-parameter support means walking up the enclosing call expression in the backend — a design step of its own.

Four smaller correctness fixes. (1) The five `analysis/*` handlers plus `analysis/check` created a temp directory per request and never removed it; with completions on the keystroke path a long session left thousands behind. All six now go through `createAnalysisWorkDir`/`deleteAnalysisWorkDir` in a `try/finally`, with the delete's own failure swallowed so it can never mask a result. (2) The five bridge-dispatch cases in `MainWindow.axaml.fs` were bare `(task { ... } :> Task) |> ignore` — a throw, a missed document lookup or an unconfigured hook left the page's pending-resolver entry alive and its Monaco promise unsettled forever (unbounded for completions). They now share one `resolveEditorRequest` helper that logs and always posts that provider's "nothing found" notification instead. (3) `documentLoaded` didn't clear the five `pending*Resolvers` maps, so a response for the previous document could be applied against the new one — `setValue` mutates the same model object in place. A `settlePending()` now resolves and drops all of them on a document switch. (4) Rename's empty result carries a `rejectReason` so Monaco says why nothing happened.

Verified: `dotnet build` clean (0 warnings, 0 errors) and full-solution `dotnet test` at 409 passed / 12 failed — the same twelve `RequiresDocker` SQL Server and PostgreSQL container tests that fail without Docker, nothing new. Worker.Tests 31 → 35 (four added: two dotted-completion, one signature-help trigger-column, one definition-filePath-rewrite). The JS changes have no automated coverage — `editor-host.html` is manual-verify-only in this environment, as every prior entry touching it notes — but each JS fix is anchored to a Worker-level test that pins the column or value it now produces.

Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Editor/EditorAssets/editor-host.html`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Worker/Handlers.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Worker/Program.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis/Completions.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/MainWindow.axaml.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Worker.Tests/HandlersTests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/ShellViewModelTests.fs`, `docs/DEVLOG.md`.

## 2026-08-11 — Fix: `CsvConnector.ReadRows()`'s `Rows` sequence could only be enumerated once

User report, found using the app for real: after registering a `test1` CSV connection and running

```fsharp
let result = Connections.csv "test1"
let rows = result.Rows |> Seq.toArray
printfn "%A" rows.[1]
```

the Results pane showed `"Some(null)"` (root cause and fix: the entry below) — but fixing that surfaced a second, real bug underneath it while building `Results.table` (also below): calling anything that enumerates `result.Rows` a second time, after the script had already called `Seq.toArray` on it once, threw `ObjectDisposedException: Cannot read from a closed TextReader`.

**Root cause.** `CsvConnector.ReadRows()` opened one `StreamReader` outside the `seq { }` block that builds `Rows`, then captured that same already-open reader inside the block via `use streamReader = streamReader`. Every `GetEnumerator()` call on `Rows` re-entered the block, but they all shared the one physical file handle — the first full enumeration read to EOF and disposed it (the `use` binding's `Dispose` firing at the end of that first pass), so a second enumeration hit a reader that was already closed. The method's own doc comment ("owns the underlying file handle until fully enumerated or disposed") described this as deliberate streaming behavior, but nothing about C.5–C.10's `ConnectorQueryResult` contract says `Rows` is single-use — `Connections.sql`/`SqlServer`/`PostgreSql`'s reader-backed sequences aren't (a fresh `SqliteDataReader`/`SqlDataReader` per `ExecuteQuery` call), and no prior test in this codebase had ever enumerated a CSV result twice to catch the difference.

**Fix.** The header row is now peeked through its own short-lived reader (opened, one record read, disposed via `use` before `ReadRows()` returns) purely to compute `Columns` eagerly. The `Rows` sequence now opens a brand-new `StreamReader` *inside* the `seq { }` block on every single enumeration, reading from the start and re-skipping the header (or treating the first record as data, per `HasHeader`) each time — genuinely re-enumerable, at the cost of a re-open per pass, which is the normal price of re-enumerating any lazy sequence and no different from what `File.ReadLines` does.

TDD: `ReadRows's Rows sequence can be enumerated more than once` (`CsvConnectorTests.fs`) — enumerate `Rows` via `Seq.toArray` twice, assert both passes return the same two rows. Failed first with the exact `ObjectDisposedException` from the real bug (not a compile error), confirming RED for the right reason; passes after the fix. Full `CsvConnectorTests.fs` (12/12) and `Connectors.Tests` (49/49) re-run clean — no regression to the quoted-field/embedded-newline/non-UTF8/no-header cases the existing suite already covered.

Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Connectors/CsvConnector.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Connectors.Tests/CsvConnectorTests.fs`.

## 2026-08-11 — Feature: `Results.table`/`Results.text` let a script control the Results pane explicitly; fixes the "Some(null)" display bug at its root

User report: running a script that ends in a `let` binding or a `printfn` (no trailing expression of its own) showed the literal text `"Some(null)"` in the Results pane, no matter what the script had actually computed earlier. Two real bugs, both fixed, plus the feature the user asked for on top.

**Bug 1 — the display bug itself.** `Projection.projectWithCursor`'s `null` case (reached whenever FSI's own last-expression value is `unit`, e.g. a script ending in `printfn`) set `DisplayText = Some "null"` — a real F# `string option`, not a null reference. `ResultsView.axaml`'s `TextBlock.Text="{Binding Result.DisplayText}"` binds straight to `ResultViewModel.DisplayText`, which returned that `string option` unwrapped. Avalonia can't implicitly convert `option` to `string` for a `TextBlock.Text` binding, so it fell back to calling `.ToString()` on the boxed `FSharpOption<string>` object itself — rendering the literal text `"Some(null)"` (the option's own structural `ToString()`, not the string `"null"` it wrapped). Fixed at both ends: `ResultViewModel.DisplayText` now returns a plain, unwrapped `string` (`Option.defaultValue ""`), and `Projection`'s `null` case now produces `ResultKind.NoValue` with no `DisplayText` at all — a genuinely empty Results pane instead of a confusing literal `"null"`, since there's nothing to show either way.

**Bug 2 — completions/rename/definition weren't the only place a bare option leaking into a binding could bite.** No other `ResultViewModel` member had the same issue (`ClipboardText` already defaulted through `Option.defaultValue ""` internally), but this is now the only place `DisplayText` is exposed, closing the gap for any future `None` case too.

**Feature.** A new `Results` module (`FSharpQueryStudio.Results`, alongside `Projection`) gives a script an explicit way to choose what the Results pane shows, instead of relying solely on FSI's own last-expression convention:

```fsharp
let result = Connections.csv "test1"
let rows = result.Rows |> Seq.toArray
Results.table result |> ignore   // shows result's own real columns, right now
printfn "%A" rows.[1]
```

`Results.table (query: ConnectorQueryResult)` shows `query`'s own real column names as a Table (bounded to the same `Projection.PreviewRowLimit`, `IsTruncated` set correctly) — bypassing generic result projection entirely, which has no way to know a raw `obj array seq`'s column names on its own and would otherwise wrap the whole thing as one opaque `"Value"` column. `Results.text (value: 'a)` shows `value`'s structured-printed form (`sprintf "%A"`, the same formatting FSI's own REPL echo uses) as text. Both return their input unchanged, so they compose in a pipeline without disturbing the script's own logic. Whichever was called most recently wins for that evaluation; `Handlers.runEvaluate` calls `Results.reset()` before every evaluation (Isolated or a Persistent session's next turn) so a previous run's push never leaks forward, and checks `Results.currentEnvelope` before falling back to the existing FSI-tail-value path.

Reachable with no `#r`/`open` of its own, same as `Connections.*` (Task 3 of the Connections UI plan) — `FsiSession.create`'s auto-reference prologue now also references and opens `FSharpQueryStudio.Results`, so `Connections.csv "test1" |> Results.table` works in a script exactly as written above.

Building the real end-to-end Worker test for this (`Connections.csv` into `Results.table`, materializing `Rows` first via `Seq.toArray` exactly like the user's script) is what surfaced Bug 2 in `CsvConnector` above — `Results.table` enumerating the same `ConnectorQueryResult.Rows` a second time after the script already had.

TDD across every layer: `Tests.fs` (`Projection`, Results.Tests) for the `null → NoValue` change; `ResultViewModelTests.fs`/`ShellViewModelTests.fs` for the unwrapped `DisplayText`, including a test asserting `vm.DisplayText` is actually typed `string`, not just equal to one; `ScriptResultsTests.fs` (new, 7 tests) for `Results.table`/`text`/`reset`/`currentEnvelope` directly; two new `HandlersTests.fs` cases proving the exact user scenario end-to-end through real dispatch — `Results.table` winning over a trailing `printfn`, and a bare `let`+`printfn` script now showing `NoValue`/no `DisplayText` instead of `"null"`.

Verified: `dotnet build` clean (0 warnings, 0 errors) across all 25 projects (`Results`/`Results.Tests` gain a `Connectors` reference, `Execution`/`Worker` unaffected beyond the new module being reachable). Full-solution `dotnet test`: every project green except the same 12 pre-existing `RequiresDocker` `Integration.Tests` failures.

Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Results/ScriptResults.fs` (new), `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Results/Projection.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Results/FSharpQueryStudio.Results.fsproj`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/ResultViewModel.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Worker/Handlers.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Execution/FsiSession.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Execution/FSharpQueryStudio.Execution.fsproj`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Results.Tests/ScriptResultsTests.fs` (new), `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Results.Tests/Tests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Results.Tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Results.Tests.fsproj`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/ResultViewModelTests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/ShellViewModelTests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Worker.Tests/HandlersTests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Integration.Tests/ExecutionServiceTests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Integration.Tests/MilestoneAAcceptanceTests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Integration.Tests/MilestoneEAcceptanceTests.fs`.

## 2026-08-11 — Fix: live analysis (Problems pane, hover, go-to-definition, signature help, completions, rename) never saw `Connections`/`Results` at all

User report: `Connections.csv "test1"` ran perfectly (worked end to end, real data back), but the Problems pane still showed `The value, namespace, type or module 'Connections' is not defined.` (and the same for `Results`), and autocomplete offered nothing for either. Called out correctly as a real bug, not something to wave off as expected — the modules plainly do exist in the running application.

**Root cause.** `FsiSession.create` (real execution) auto-`#r`s and `open`s both assemblies before a script ever runs, so `Connections.sql`/`Results.table` just work with no boilerplate. `AnalysisContext.build` (live diagnostics/hover/definition/signature-help/completions/rename — all six share this one context-construction function) never got the same treatment: it builds its own, completely separate FCS project options, with no reference to either assembly at all. A script that ran fine was still being *checked* against a project that had never heard of either module — genuinely two different execution/analysis environments quietly diverging, not a benign inconsistency.

**First attempt, wrong.** Marked both `Connections` and `Results` `[<AutoOpen>]`, then appended `-r:<dll path>` entries to `AnalysisContext.build`'s already-returned `FSharpProjectOptions.OtherOptions`. Neither piece did what was needed: `[<AutoOpen>]` makes a module's own *members* resolve unqualified (`sql`, `table`, ...) — not the module's own qualified name (`Connections.sql`, exactly how the architecture spec's own example and every script in this codebase actually calls it), so it was solving a problem nobody had while leaving the real one (`Connections`/`Results` as identifiers) unfixed. And appending to `OtherOptions` *after* `GetProjectOptionsFromScript` returns is silently never consulted by `ParseAndCheckFileInProject` for script-resolution-rules checking — confirmed empirically (a dedicated `AnalysisContextTests` case proved the appended reference paths were present in `context.References`, while `Checker.check`ing a `Connections.sql`-calling script kept reporting "not defined" regardless). The correct hook is `GetProjectOptionsFromScript`'s own `otherFlags` parameter, which *is* honored — it's fed into the same resolution call that derives every other reference, rather than bolted on afterward. Both wrong pieces (`[<AutoOpen>]`, the post-hoc `OtherOptions` append) were reverted; `otherFlags` replaced them.

**`otherFlags` alone still wasn't enough.** A reference makes `Connections`/`Results`'s *members* resolvable through a qualified path, but the bare identifiers `Connections`/`Results` still need their *enclosing namespaces* (`FSharpQueryStudio.Connectors`/`FSharpQueryStudio.Results`) opened — no FCS compiler flag does that; the only reliable mechanism is textually prepending `open` statements to whatever gets checked, the same thing `FsiSession.create` already does for real execution. Textually prepending shifts every line number in the checked text, though, and all six analysis functions convert FCS's line numbers to/from the wire's 0-based coordinates using the caller's own, unshifted numbering — get this wrong and every diagnostic, hover result, and rename edit lands on the wrong line.

**Fix.** `AnalysisContext` gained `sourceForCheck context source` (prepends a fixed 2-line `open FSharpQueryStudio.Connectors\nopen FSharpQueryStudio.Results\n` prologue) and `lineOffset context` (the 2-line correction every caller applies). Every one of the six analysis functions now: (1) checks `sourceForCheck`'d text instead of raw `source`; (2) adds `lineOffset` to the caller's own 1-based `line` before any FCS lookup call; (3) subtracts `lineOffset` from any FCS-reported line for a location that resolved *inside this same checked file* — never applied to a location FCS resolves elsewhere (`GoToDefinition`'s cross-file case, `FSharp.Core`, another project file, which has its own real, unshifted lines). `Checker.check` additionally filters out anything at or below the prologue's own two lines, so a hypothetical future prologue diagnostic could never leak to the user as if it were their own.

**Real regression the naive version would have caused, caught before it shipped.** The offset is *not* a bare constant — it's `AnalysisContext.lineOffset context`, `0` for an E.5 multi-file `.fsproj` context (`buildForProject`) and `2` for a standalone script (`build`). The prologue has no per-file story for a multi-file project (only the one file being live-edited would ever get prefixed; every other project file stays on disk, unprefixed) — applying it unconditionally broke the one pre-existing multi-file `GoToDefinitionTests` case immediately (a real regression, caught by the existing suite, not shipped). Scoping the offset to `context.ProjectOptions.UseScriptResolutionRules` fixed it without touching multi-file behavior at all; that test passes unmodified.

TDD across every one of the six functions: a `Checker.check`-level test asserting `Connections.sql "Sales" "select 1" [] |> Results.table |> ignore` now produces zero diagnostics (the Problems-pane bug directly); two `CompletionsTests` cases asserting real semantic (tier-2) completions on `Connections.`/`Results.` include actual members (`sql`, `csv`, `table`, `text`) — proving autocomplete, not just that the static tier-1 string list happens to mention `Connections.sql`; one new case each in `HoverTests`/`GoToDefinitionTests`/`SignatureHelpTests`/`RenameTests` resolving `Connections.sql` with no `#r`/`open` of its own; an `AnalysisContextTests` case asserting the reference set includes both assemblies. All written RED-first — the first three failed with the exact "not defined" error from the real bug, the completion cases with an empty result list, before any fix landed.

Verified: `dotnet build` clean (0 warnings, 0 errors). `Analysis.Tests`: 42 → 50 (8 new, all passing, including the pre-existing multi-file `GoToDefinitionTests` case unmodified and still green). Full-solution `dotnet test`: everything green except the same 12 pre-existing `RequiresDocker` `Integration.Tests` failures.

Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis/AnalysisContext.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis/Checker.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis/DiagnosticMapping.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis/Hover.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis/GoToDefinition.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis/SignatureHelp.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis/Rename.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis/Completions.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis.fsproj`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis.Tests/AnalysisContextTests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis.Tests/DiagnosticMappingTests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis.Tests/CompletionsTests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis.Tests/HoverTests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis.Tests/GoToDefinitionTests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis.Tests/SignatureHelpTests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Analysis.Tests/RenameTests.fs`.

## 2026-08-11 — Feature: real F# syntax highlighting — Monaco ships no F# language of its own

User report: the editor showed no syntax colors at all. Root cause was exactly what `editor-host.html`'s own comment already admitted, unaddressed since the very first editor-integration task: `monaco.languages.register({ id: "fsharp" })` registers the language *name* only — no tokenizer, so Monaco falls back to plain, uncolored text. Unlike TypeScript/JavaScript/Python/HTML/CSS, F# isn't one of Monaco's bundled `vs/basic-languages` — confirmed by listing the vendored `EditorAssets/vs/basic-languages` directory directly rather than assuming, no F#/OCaml/ML entry exists. A real tokenizer had to be hand-written; there's nothing to enable.

**What was built.** A Monarch tokenizer (Monaco's own declarative lexer format) covering F#'s actual lexical grammar, not a generic keyword list: the real reserved-word table (`abstract`/`and`/`as`/... through `while`/`with`/`yield`, including the less common `sig`/`upcast`/`downcast`/`inline`/`inherit` ones); bang-suffixed control-flow forms (`let!`/`use!`/`do!`/`yield!`/`return!`/`match!`) matched as single keyword tokens rather than falling out as keyword-plus-stray-operator; the full operator set (`|>`, `<|`, `->`, `<-`, `::`, `:>`, `:?>`, ...); four distinct string forms, each genuinely different lexically — plain `"..."` with backslash escapes, triple-quoted `"""..."""`, verbatim `@"..."` (only `""` is an escape, backslashes are literal), and interpolated `$"...{expr}..."` with each `{...}` splice re-entering the normal tokenizer rules (via Monarch's state-stack `@push`/`@pop`) so a spliced identifier or nested record literal still colors correctly instead of vanishing into the surrounding string color; nestable `(* ... *)` block comments (real F# semantics — `(* (* ... *) ... *)` doesn't close early) alongside `//` line comments; hex/octal/binary/float numeric literals with every real F# suffix (`UL`, `uy`, `f`, `M`, `I`, ...); `[<Attribute>]` blocks tokenized as their own class while still re-tokenizing whatever's inside them as real code; and a PascalCase-starts-a-type-or-module heuristic (F#'s own naming convention) so `Connections`/`ConnectorQueryResult`/etc. read as types without needing semantic information. A matching `languageConfiguration` (comment tokens, bracket pairs, auto-closing pairs) rides along for free editor behavior — Ctrl+/ comment toggling, matching-bracket highlighting, auto-closed quotes — none of which existed before either.

**One real bug caught before it could ship, found by structural self-review since no browser was available this session to render it directly (see Verification below).** Monarch's generic bracket rule (`[/[{}()\[\]]/, "@brackets"]`) relies on the tokenizer's *own* `brackets` array to know what each bracket pair's token class is — a completely different thing from the `languageConfiguration.brackets` array (which only drives the editor's bracket-matching/auto-close UI behavior, not tokenizer output). The first draft only set the latter; every `{`, `[`, `(` in a real script would have rendered with no color and no bracket-depth tracking at all — silently, no error, just flat gray brackets in an otherwise colorful file. Caught by writing a small Node harness that stubs out `monaco`/`document`/`window` enough to `eval` the real script and walk `fsharpMonarchLanguage.tokenizer`'s actual object structure, rather than trusting the code by inspection alone. Fixed by adding the tokenizer-level `brackets` array Monarch's `@brackets` action actually consults.

**Verification, and its real limit.** `node --version` confirmed available in this environment (unlike Python, tried first and not installed) — used to (1) confirm the whole inline `<script>` block still parses as valid JavaScript after the change (`new Function(source)`), and (2) the harness above, which evaluates the real tokenizer/language-configuration object literals in a stubbed environment and walks every tokenizer state confirming each rule is a well-formed `[RegExp, action]`/`[RegExp, action, nextState]` tuple or a valid `{include: ...}` directive — 8 states (`root`, `attribute`, `string`, `stringTriple`, `stringVerbatim`, `stringInterpolated`, `interpolation`, `comment`), zero structural problems. Attempted to go further and actually render the page: served `EditorAssets/` over a local Node HTTP server (Python unavailable) and reached for the `claude-in-chrome` browser tools to load `editor-host.html` and look at real, colored output — the Chrome extension wasn't connected this session, so that step didn't happen. What's verified is that the tokenizer is syntactically valid and structurally sound against Monarch's own schema; what's *not* verified is what a human eye would actually see — same standing limitation as every other `editor-host.html` change in this project's history, never fully closed until someone opens the real running app.

Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Editor/EditorAssets/editor-host.html`.

## 2026-08-11 — Feature: remove a registered connection from the Workspace panel

User report/request: no way to remove a connection once registered — flagged as a real, known gap during the Connections UI plan's own final review (re-registering a name always minted a fresh entry, orphaning the old broken one with no UI to reach it) but deliberately deferred as follow-up work at the time. Built now, matching every layer `RegisterConnection` already established.

**Storage.** `Workspace.removeConnection workspaceDir id` — filters the matching `Id` out of the persisted list and rewrites `workspace.json`; a no-op, not an error, for an `Id` that isn't registered (already removed, or never existed — the caller can't tell the two apart and shouldn't need to, same convention `registerConnection` already uses for its own replace-by-`Id` case).

**Worker.** New `ConnectionRemoveParams = { Id: Guid }` (Protocol) and a `"connection/remove"` dispatch case, `handleConnectionRemove`, mirroring `handleConnectionRegister` exactly — persists via `Workspace.removeConnection`, returns the updated full list. The worker itself never touches the OS credential store, same boundary `connection/register` already draws (secrets are the App layer's job, workspace.json is the worker's).

**App.** `ConnectionService.remove` deletes the connection's own credential from `ISecretStore` *first* (if it had one — `SecretReference |> Option.iter secretStore.Delete`), then asks the worker to drop the `workspace.json` entry — the reverse order from `register`'s save-then-send, and deliberately so: a crash or dropped call between the two steps this way leaves an orphaned *credential* (harmless, invisible, cleanable later) rather than a `workspace.json` entry pointing at a `SecretReference` nothing will ever read again. This also closes a real, separate gap the earlier final-branch review flagged and parked: `ISecretStore.Delete` existed since Milestone D but had no caller anywhere in the running app until now.

**UI.** `ShellViewModel.RemoveConnectionAsync`/`RemoveConnection`, identical shape to `RegisterConnectionAsync`/`RegisterConnection` — updates `Connections` and re-raises `PropertyChanged` on success, no-ops if unconfigured. `WorkspacePanel`'s `ConnectionsList` item template gained a small "×" button per row. Wiring it hit a real structural constraint the `NewConnectionMenuItem`/`confirmDiscardDirty` patterns don't: every other event handler in `MainWindow.axaml.fs` is a `do`-block closure wired via `FindControl(...).Click.Add(...)` against one, singly-named control — but a `ListBox` item template's button is a *new* control instance per row, with no single name to `FindControl`. The only way to reach the right row is XAML's own declarative `Click="RemoveConnectionClicked"`, which needs a real class member (not a closure) — the same reason `ResultsView.axaml.fs`'s `ProblemDoubleTapped` already exists as one. Added `MainWindow.RemoveConnectionClicked`, reading the clicked button's own `DataContext` (that row's `ConnectionDefinition`) the same way `ProblemDoubleTapped` reads its own row's `ProblemViewModel`. No confirmation prompt before removing — unlike closing a dirty tab, this destroys no unsaved work; re-registering the same connection is one dialog away, a deliberate, disclosed scope cut rather than an oversight.

TDD end to end: three `Workspace.removeConnection` cases (drops the match, preserves everything else, no-ops on an unknown `Id`), two Worker dispatch cases (drops via a real register-then-remove round trip, preserves the other registered connection), three `ShellViewModel` cases (calls the hook and updates `Connections`, no-op when unconfigured, `PropertyChanged` fires only on success) — all written RED-first, each failing to compile (member/function not yet defined) before the real code landed. `MainWindow.axaml.fs`'s own piece isn't unit-testable in this codebase (same standing limitation as every other XAML/code-behind change) — verified instead by a clean `dotnet build`, which is itself real signal here: the XAML compiler resolves `Click="RemoveConnectionClicked"` against the real class member at build time, so a typo or missing member would have failed the build, not just gone unnoticed until runtime.

Verified: `dotnet build` clean (0 warnings, 0 errors). `Storage.Tests` 31 → 34, `Worker.Tests` 37 → 39, `Ui.Tests` 115 → 118. Full-solution `dotnet test`: everything green except the same 12 pre-existing `RequiresDocker` `Integration.Tests` failures.

Files changed: `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Storage/Workspace.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Protocol/Connections.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Worker/Handlers.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.App/ConnectionService.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.App/App.axaml.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/ShellViewModel.fs`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/MainWindow.axaml`, `src/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui/MainWindow.axaml.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Storage.Tests/Tests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Worker.Tests/HandlersTests.fs`, `tests/FSharpQueryStudio.Ui.Tests/ShellViewModelTests.fs`.

## 2026-08-11 — Feature: real object navigation for the Workspace panel — Connection → Tables → Columns, backed by genuine schema discovery

User request: "according with the kind of workspace item, add a navigation and column labels to know what we can use from that. Also, add an object mapping to the workspace items to have real object navigation." The Workspace panel had been a flat list of connection names since the Connections UI plan shipped; `IConnector.DiscoverSchemaAsync` (real, per-connector schema discovery — SQLite/SQL Server/PostgreSQL query their own catalogs, Excel treats each sheet as a table, CSV synthesizes one table named after the file, RestJson always returns no tables since it has no fixed schema) had existed since Milestone [REST/Excel/CSV connectors] but was never reachable from the UI. Wired end to end, one layer at a time, same shape every prior connection feature this session used.

**Protocol.** `ConnectionSchemaParams = { Id: Guid }`, `SchemaColumn = { Name: string; DataType: string }`, `SchemaTable = { Name: string; Columns: SchemaColumn list }`, `ConnectionSchemaResult = { Tables: SchemaTable list }` — deliberate wire-facing mirrors of `Connectors.ConnectorColumn`/`ConnectorTable`, not reuses, since `Protocol` must never depend on `Connectors` (the same boundary `SchemaTable` for connection removal params already respects).

**Worker.** `handleConnectionSchema` + `"connection/schema"` dispatch: resolves the registered connection by `Id` through the same `WorkspaceConnectionResolver` `runEvaluate` already uses for `Connections.*` scripts, calls the connector's own `DiscoverSchemaAsync`, maps to the wire shape. A `connection/unknown` error for an `Id` nothing is registered under; a `connection/schemaFailed` error — not an unhandled exception taking the read loop down — for a real discovery failure (bad credentials, unreachable server, missing file), since unlike `Connections.sql` this runs before any script exists to catch its own exception.

**App.** `ConnectionService.schema`, a thin `Task<Result<SchemaTable list, string>>` wrapper, same shape as `register`/`remove` — real I/O stays in the worker process, this just relays the worker's own structured error message rather than silently returning an empty table list, so the panel can show the user *why* nothing came back.

**UI.** `WorkspaceColumnViewModel`/`WorkspaceTableViewModel` (thin wrappers; `WorkspaceColumnViewModel.Label` is the `"Name: DataType"` string the XAML actually shows) and `WorkspaceConnectionViewModel`, which owns the lazy-load state machine: `LoadSchemaAsync()` is idempotent once a load has *succeeded* (re-expanding an already-loaded connection never re-fetches), but a *failed* load is deliberately not sticky — calling it again retries, since the failure (bad credentials, unreachable file) may no longer apply by the next expand. `HasSchema` is `false` for `RestJson` (skips even attempting a load, since `DiscoverSchemaAsync` always returns nothing for it). `LoadError` is exposed only as `HasError`/`ErrorMessage` (a plain, already-unwrapped `string`), deliberately following the `ResultViewModel.DisplayText` lesson from earlier this session — binding a raw `string option` straight to a XAML `TextBlock.Text` falls back to Avalonia calling `.ToString()` on the boxed `FSharpOption<string>`, rendering the literal text `"Some(...)"`/`"None"` instead of unwrapping it.

`ShellViewModel.WorkspaceItems` mirrors `Connections` as these real navigation objects, reconciled by `Id` at every point that already calls `raiseConnectionsChanged` (`RegisterConnection`, `RemoveConnection`, `SeedConnections`) — reusing existing `WorkspaceConnectionViewModel` instances rather than rebuilding the list from scratch, so an already-expanded, already-loaded connection's schema survives an unrelated register/remove elsewhere in the list instead of silently resetting. `ShellViewModel.LoadConnectionSchemaAsync` is the composition-root hook, same no-op-when-unconfigured shape as every other hook here; `App.axaml.fs` wires it to `ConnectionService.schema`.

`MainWindow.axaml`'s `WorkspacePanel` gained real hierarchy: `ScrollViewer` > `ItemsControl` of `WorkspaceItems`, each rendered as an outer `Expander` (header: kind, name, the existing "×" remove button) whose content is a loading/error/empty message plus a nested `ItemsControl` of per-table `Expander`s, each showing its `Columns`' `Label` in a monospace font. Chose nested `Expander`s over Avalonia's `TreeView`/`TreeDataTemplate` deliberately — lower implementation risk given this session's standing inability to visually verify (`claude-in-chrome` unavailable all session; see below), and `Expander.IsExpanded`/`Expanding` are simple, well-known, directly-bindable/wireable. Lazy loading is triggered by `Expander.Expanding` (a real Avalonia event, fired before the expand completes) rather than a `IsExpanded` two-way-bound VM property — same reasoning `RemoveConnectionClicked` already established for why a per-row dynamically-created control needs a real class member, not a `do`-block closure: `MainWindow.ConnectionExpanderExpanded` reads the clicked `Expander`'s own `DataContext` and calls `LoadSchemaAsync()`, fire-and-forget, exactly like `RemoveConnectionClicked` calls `RemoveConnection`.

**Structural record-shape collision, twice more this session.** `SchemaTable`/`SchemaColumn` colliding with `Connectors.ConnectorTable`/`ConnectorColumn` (the same class of bug as `ConnectionSchemaParams`/`ConnectionRemoveParams` and `AnalysisRenameParams`/`AnalysisCompletionParams` earlier this session) hit `ExcelConnector.fs`, `CsvConnector.fs`, `PostgreSqlConnector.fs`, `SqlServerConnector.fs`, `HandlersTests.fs`, and `ConnectionService.fs` — fixed with explicit type ascriptions (`({ Name = x; DataType = y }: ConnectorColumn)` etc.) at every construction site, now the fourth confirmed instance of this bug class. Separately, and new this time: `DiagnosticSeverity`'s own `Error` case (`type DiagnosticSeverity = | Error | Warning | Information`, `Protocol/Diagnostics.fs`) is in scope everywhere `open FSharpQueryStudio.Protocol` is, and shadows `FSharp.Core`'s `Result.Error` — `ConnectionService.schema`'s `None, Some workerError -> return Error workerError.Message` compiled to a nonsensical "`DiagnosticSeverity` is not a function" error instead of building a `Result`. Fixed with an explicit `Result.Error` at both sites this bit (`ConnectionService.fs`, `WorkspaceConnectionViewModel.LoadSchemaAsync`, and the test that constructs a failing loader). Worth remembering for any future code in this codebase that pattern-matches on `Result` while `FSharpQueryStudio.Protocol` is open.

TDD across every layer, RED-first: two `HandlersTests.fs` cases for `connection/schema` (a real SQLite database created in a temp dir, discovered end to end through real dispatch; a structured error for an unregistered `Id`) — confirmed RED via a genuine compile error before `handleConnectionSchema` existed, not re-verified GREEN until the `Result.Error`/`DiagnosticSeverity.Error` collision above was found and fixed.
